# LESSONS for general BH (append-only; format: lane: lesson — node id)
gq-infinite-primes: A Leavitt pair makes unstable K-theory as good as stable: refinement absorbs stabilization, so kernels die in one step and surjective => injective (K_1 and K_2). With a compression lemma (every x != 0 has yxz = 1) and Khanh's frame complex, K_2(N,R) = K_2(R) for fp purely infinite simple Q-algebras, and the word problem of St_N(R) reduces to a computable parametrization of stable K_2. So for Steinberg hosts the obstruction is never unstable K_2; it is the rational scalars (Leavitt => Lambda_N = Q^x, PE_N never fp) and the shell gates — leavitt-resolvent-k2-stability-holds, leavitt-tuples-make-the-scalar-clauses-agree
bh-verify-W: Zaremsky's type (A) criterion needs only fp global structure group + finitely many orbits on PAIRS of splitting directions + fg direction stabilizers + a Cohen–Macaulay splitting nerve; the nerve can be a finite-field subspace lattice (Solomon–Tits), not just a simplex. Splittings of Z_l^D see only X/lX, so Z-valued pair invariants that kill discrete vector actions vanish. Design hosts for new inputs as compact spaces whose splittings factor through a finite geometry; the germ-cocycle quasi-retraction is the first necessary test — twisted-integral-affine-full-group-is-finitely-presented (gq-bh-bh-verify-W-report, b2b4b26f6)
bh-refute: Carry hosts in B_A, not just BH: every host construction whose proof checks Zaremsky's oligomorphic criterion (BBMZ full contracting RSGs) is a type (A) actor, and only B_A has the product / graph-product / wreath closures; recording "type (A)" upgrades a single embedding into a closure theorem — hyperbolic-groups-lie-in-the-permutational-class (verified 9650d7a6b)
bh-refute: Profinite density of an infinite-index subgroup Λ ≤ Γ makes every A wr_{Λ\Γ} Γ non-residually-finite (a_o a_{og}^{-1} dies in every finite quotient); so residually finite / self-similar / automaton host classes are blind to permutational wreaths over dense cosets, and those inputs force non-RF (Thompson-like) hosts — z-wr-psl2-z-half-is-not-residually-finite (verified 216e86071)
bh-refute: Torsion and arithmetic "flux" are absorbed by Zappa–Szép k-graph full groups: finite cube stabilizers become a germ groupoid, Li's condition (F) reduces to faithfulness of germ classes on boxes (on ∂Ω only vertex boundaries occur), and translations by Z act as odometers; one mechanism covers tree lattices with torsion and RCWA(Z) — cocompact-tree-product-lattices-lie-in-type-a-class, rcwa-groups-satisfy-boone-higman (verified 6560d9ba1, 8c7e2dbc7)
bh-refute: A universal quantifier over a whole group does not survive embedding, but one over a cyclic subgroup does; the core of a Mihailova fibre product in a mapping torus of F×F encodes "∀n p(ψ^n W)=1" inside a finitely presented decidable group, turning Π_1/Π_2 patterns into mixed identities — fp-decidable-group-whose-e-is-not-recursively-presented (verified 4067f5db5)
bh-refute: Periodic transition matrices are no obstruction to k-graph host routes: restrict to one cyclic class and take the skew product over L/nZ^k (L = class-preserving degrees); 'primitive' weakens to 'irreducible' throughout — type-preserving-vertex-free-a2-groups-embed-in-fp-simple-groups (verified)
bh-near-actions: Arithmetic compactness is a finite-nucleus machine. For piecewise-affine maps with slopes in ⟨β⟩ and translations in Z[β], coded by the β-shift, each deep local action is (state, slope defect, offset). The defect is uniformly bounded by a conjugate-growth length lemma, and the offsets lie in Z[β] with bounded conjugates, a finite set when β is Pisot. So Pisot + (F) turns "algebraic data" into a contracting RSG, hence into a BH host that is itself finitely presented. The only field restriction is a sign-parity condition on units (Q(√3) fails it), and the computability of the module is necessary. — pisot-stein-groups-are-contracting-rsgs, f-unit-fields-are-characterized-by-isolated-sign-units, stein-derived-groups-need-computable-modules
bh-free-50: Periodic groups in Thompson-type hosts need depth visible at bounded range: either unbounded-reach elements (Röver–Nekrashevych tails) or graded-rigid dynamics (odometer phase non-open at holes). nV's universal machines (SMART) have one free cell per level, so their phases are open off two orbits: universality and graded periodicity pull apart in nV — smart-level-zero-return-map-has-free-divergence
bh-testcases: Higher-rank MIF replaces "distinct" by "opposite": in ANY locally finite thick Euclidean building, strongly regular elements have uniform north–south dynamics on Ch(∂X). So PBH (hence every BH closure property) for a higher-rank fp simple group reduces to two lattice inputs: dense regular poles and non-domesticity at infinity, the latter being an irreducibility condition along singular (panel-tree) directions — strongly-regular-building-elements-are-opposition-proximal, titz-witzel-kernel-elements-are-nowhere-domestic-at-infinity (Attempt 5).
bh-testcases: For simple factors, the only type [A_2] actions of S×T are products of factor actions or the left-right action (Goursat), so product closure cannot come from the product itself; it needs an enlarged actor — product-of-simple-groups-type-a-actions-come-from-factors.
bh-refute: Full-group hosts preserve geometry for free: a locally determined label (e.g. a locally faithful action) makes the input undistorted in every f.g. overgroup inside its full group and in SV_Γ, so BBMZ/Belk–Zaremsky BH embeddings of locally faithful actors are automatically QI — hyperbolic-groups-undistortedly-embed-in-fp-simple-groups (verified)
bh-algebra: In Leavitt–Nekrashevych/RN envelopes three things separate. (1) Finite presentation is free: any recursion on a free or fp base gives an fp completion whose base is the self-similar quotient B/J, so the input need not be fp. (2) Complexity lives in the time-to-die: J is not injective, and the complexity fence only covers injective recursions. (3) Simplicity is exactly "contraction to constants": every nonzero element has a unit descendant fixed diagonally. With scalar constants K this forces the input to be residually finite-dimensional, the algebra face of the RF wall. A constant field (F_p(t)) or a constant simple algebra (B ⊗ L) removes it. So the BH lever is the choice of constant object the recursion contracts to — noninjective-ln-completions-have-self-similar-bases, scalar-descendants-make-completions-simple, scalar-descendant-hosts-see-only-rfd-inputs

## bh-free-30 (09-18): the mass-module obstruction (no-free-q-odd-action-by-v-has-a-doubling-intertwiner)
- **Chain of forcing.** Divisible inputs (Q-roots of one element) force free Z/p torsion for infinitely many p on a transverse coordinate. That torsion forces 1/p into the value set of every invariant mass on clopen sets.
- **What self-similarity does.** A V-intertwiner with a doubled lift makes the cone masses of a Markov–Kakutani fixed-point measure a finitely generated Z[1/d]-module. So only finitely many primes fit, and Q ⋊ ⟨2⟩ is not in 2V_τ with 1 ↦ odometer, for any action, exotic or not.
- **Quick test for any candidate host.** Is the invariant-mass (trace) range on the carrying coordinate infinitely generated over Z[1/d]? Tree-self-similar hosts fail it.
- **Cross-route.** It is the same tension as the ring gate's "[1] divisible in K_0 versus a finitely generated K-budget". Hosts for GL_n(Q) need infinitely many independent scaling ratios that are not tied together by a self-similar map.
bh-lattices: Comparison and type (A) are free. For any ample groupoid coded by a finite degree category with strongly connected pieces (arbitrary unit groups), the Cuntz factor makes any two nonempty clopen sets in a piece equivalent (refine plus duplicate), so a finitely presented stabilized full group is a type (A) host. BH in PBH form thus reduces to (1) a faithful finitely coded Cantor action and (2) Li-type finite presentation. Finite unit groups cap the word problem at exponential time, so universal reach needs complex unit groups. — finitely-coded-cantor-actions-give-type-a-hosts
bh-lattices: Every free finite cover of a Cantor boundary is a skew product, and stabilizing by O_2 cannot untwist (continuous cohomology is a retract; Matui homology vanishing is about measures). Boundary-built hosts therefore never reach a central extension whose class survives on point stabilizers, e.g. on flat tori. — central-extension-untwisting-over-boundaries
bh-shell-kazhdan: The shell germ gate is self-similarity at infinity. Rigidity kills it at window zero: FW transfixes the graph of the shift, so the index vanishes and Kazhdan inputs need window N ≥ 1 (fw-inputs-pass-window-zero-only-via-proper-self-embeddings). Realized on Z_2, the gate becomes an odometer-stable automaton group, so the shell route and the Röver–Nekrashevych route merge (odometer-conjugation-realizes-the-shell-shift).
- [bh-free-36, 7903d9d1b] cubulated-groups-act-by-bisections-of-a-pointed-cube-category: hosts must match the rank of the input's flats. Rank-one V-germ codings fail for any group with product regions, because one element shifts independent hyperplane directions by different amounts. Geodesics modulo square flips have a finite local concatenation state (a cube: last-edge hyperplanes pairwise cross), so every cocompact cube-complex action is a finite-state action by bisections of a finite-object higher-rank category. BH for cubulated groups then reduces to Li's finiteness conditions for that one category.
- bh-typeA-universal: Twisted Brin–Thompson simplicity costs no finiteness: for faithful actions SV_G is F_n ⟺ (A_n) ⟺ Z≀_S G is F_n, so F_n/fp simple envelopes via SV = actors with F_n/fp lamplighters; Brown-criterion "bad simplex stabilizer" obstructions dissolve under the interval filtration (only grid intervals count, in degree = colors spent) — twisted-brin-thompson-type-fn-criterion
- bh-typeA-universal: Stabilizers do not survive passing to an action through a quotient (the kernel lies in all of them); FFWZ's non-faithful fp proof has this gap, so the relative-PBH route needs repair — ffwz-nonfaithful-stein-stabilizers-contain-canonical-kernel
bh-pal-kourovka59: Proving that transpositions generate a box-groupoid full group is pure counting. Swaps preserve the tree type, factorization squares trade one p-split for (p−1) 2-splits, and stable refinement cancels; so an explicit group like CT_P(Z) is certified equal to its full-group host (and inherits simplicity and fp) by an elementary type count, the shadow of H_0⊗Z/2 = H_1 = 0. Room always comes from refinement; no relations come for free — piecewise-canonical-permutations-are-transposition-products (0e0c97d15)
bh-houghton-presentation: For permutation groups containing FSym, finiteness is governed by finite-set stabilizers, not germs. (A×C)·FSym inherits F_m from them via the contractible balanced slice of the infinite recoloring cube, so H_n(G) is F_m whenever strongly shift-similar G is. Houghton-like envelopes cannot repair a non-fp envelope through its germs. — finitary-mixing-preserves-type-f-m, strongly-shift-similar-type-f-m-passes-to-houghton-like-groups
bh-houghton-presentation: Conjugating by the index-one shift at infinity preserves orbit and end counts of germs, while x^k multiplies ends by k. So no BS(1,k) (|k|≥2) near-acts on N with t as the shift, and a one-ray near action cannot realize an ascending HNN self-embedding cyclically. End counting is a cheap obstruction for near-action hosts. — no-nontrivial-germ-is-shift-conjugate-to-a-proper-power
bh-free-45: In the full-group / twisted Brin–Thompson route, both of Zaremsky's refinements reduce to the full group ALONE. Undistortion is free: locally faithful input ⇒ subadditive local label ⇒ undistorted in the full closure, and the acting group quasi-retracts from SV. Finite-set stabilizers of full groups with virtually cyclic germs are iterated ascending HNN extensions of the same full groups on clopen subsets, so SV is F_n once the full-group family is F_n, and only if the group itself is. So QI+F_∞ Boone–Higman = "find an F_n full group containing G"; when a single F_∞ host of the building blocks exists (2V for RAAGs), all closures inherit it — full-group-label-length-bounds-distortion, twisted-bt-of-full-contracting-rsg-inherits-f-n-from-rsg-family, virtually-compact-special-groups-undistorted-in-2v
gq-typeA-design: FW turns near-equivariance into exact equivariance (the graph of a near-equivariant map is commensurated, FW transfixes it). So for Kazhdan inputs the shell germ gate at window zero, and at every commuting window, is an INTRINSIC question: does P (or [P,P]) have a faithful self-similar action of finite defect, X ≅ X − Y twisted by an injective endomorphism? The ascending direction never occurs, the centre must have finite exponent, and defect one forces high transitivity with a trivial-core endomorphism of infinite index. — fw-window-zero-shells-are-self-similar-actions (30955c632)
bh-free-18: Ascending HNN extensions of Z in Brin–Thompson hosts = branching numbers of self-similar one-head hierarchies: exact m-fold nesting with zero-cost first/last sub-moves gives a height-m renormalization, hence BS(1,m) (all odd m via SMART_m); even m is a parity problem (crossings split oddly), not a host problem — bs-1-m-embeds-in-brin-thompson-3v-for-odd-m.
bh-free-18: Dynamical power-conjugacy symmetrizes (T on one cone, T^-1 on the other, swap the cones): BS(1,m) hosts give BS(1,-m) for free, so obstructions see only |m| — renormalizable-thompson-elements-give-negative-baumslag-solitar.
bh-free-18: Renormalization identities of finite-table elements of kV are LOCAL (bounded return + bounded reading radius), so a lazy exhaustive DFS with symbolic unread cells certifies them on all configurations in seconds; use it to certify, not sample, table-built host embeddings — research/artifacts/gq-bh-bh-free-18-odd-smart-certificate.md.
bh-free-54: For any group with a faithful piecewise-integral-projective action (Mod(S_g) on PML, GL_n(Z)), the full clopen-transitive host [[GL_{m+1}(Z) ⋉ Ŝ_m]] is FREE, and by Thumann its F_∞ is a finite-type question about ONE local operad (unimodular dissections), the same for every such group. That operad is very likely not finitely generated in dim 2 (primes at sizes 2,3,6,9–15), so the Serret gate (a group-invariant coding) and the fp gate are ONE gate: a finite-type coding the group preserves (unimodular-dissection-operad-has-finite-type, rational-projective-full-groups-fp-via-thumann-operad-finiteness).
bh-free-22: Finite presentation of a Thompson-like host is local: fp <V, x_1..x_k> (x_i V-local off finitely many points) is pinned down by its short relations, so any deep perturbation invisible to short words proves non-fp. RF via independent finite quotients per depth is invisible to short relations; a BH host must carry a finite rule transporting depth n to depth n+1 (cross-depth germ relations; self-similarity is the known one) — annular-free-product-germ-hosts-are-not-finitely-presented
bh-free-10: Combination closure of B_A (amalgams/HNN) never needs new actors. Every edge reduces to a realization move in ONE overgroup: identity edges ⟺ the edge group is a centralizer; twisted finite edges ⟺ conjugacy ⟺ one swap of A×A. In full Cantor hosts, conjugacy comes from gluing fundamental domains, and the only obstruction is the stabilizer-type set of the finite subgroups. — pbh-coset-wreaths-iff-identity-edge-hnns, pbh-finite-edge-closure-equivalences, finite-subgroups-of-full-cantor-groups-conjugacy-criterion
bh-kazhdan-inputs: The germ method is LOCAL. A finitely presented finite-singularity extension of any tame base (V, nV, F, T, ...) has finitely presented germ groups at every point, by any proof; and a rigid (FW/Kazhdan) input is forced faithfully into ONE singular germ group, which it cannot virtually normalize the local dilation of. So germ-type BH = realizing each simple Kazhdan input in one fp germ group Q ⊇ <S, τ> plus the SingFix assembly; the host's global shape adds nothing. — fp-germ-extensions-of-locally-moving-bases-have-fp-germ-groups, simple-kazhdan-in-germ-extension-of-v-sits-in-one-germ-group, normal-germ-extensions-of-v-have-only-finite-kazhdan-subgroups
- [bh-free-25, 8c7c0e114/d2c0ee983] affine-self-similar-groups-are-function-field-linear + two-counter-kms-top-has-no-affine-self-similar-action + kms-configuration-modules-have-trivial-coinvariants: self-similarity is an ETALE condition. Sections give Z = Z(0) + X·P(Z), so linear/affine self-similar hosts are linear over a global function field (Krull dim 1, poly-time WP). Lamp-digit hosts need nonzero coinvariants. So self-similar hosts can reach hard or higher-dimensional inputs (KMS, C_p wr Z^2) only through nonlinear (carry) sections: the complexity root needs non-algebraic section data.
bh-algebra: Retraction wall. If a self-similar base contracts to its simple constants S and retracts onto S, every simple input PLACED IN THE BASE already lies in M_n(S). Since BH reduces to simple inputs, recursions help them only through MIXING relations (J leaving the ideal of the transducer letters) or by placing the input in the completion outside the base (as Leavitt letters host UHF). General-BH envelopes must get their power from non-retracting bases or off-base placement, not from base self-similarity alone — retracting-constant-hosts-add-only-residual-inputs
bh-universal: **Linear type (A).** Stein–Farley finiteness sees a structure group only through its mod-ℓ split geometry. So groups that are hopeless as permutation groups (E(Z) on vectors, with Z-valued pair invariants) still give fp simple hosts when they act linearly on Z_ℓ^D and are oligomorphic mod ℓ (`affine-type-a-structure-groups-give-fp-simple-hosts`; one host for all GL_n(Z[1/P])). The reach is sharp: saturation forces arithmetic rings (`saturated-l-adic-subrings-are-arithmetic`), so t-variables and all-primes inputs need other local models.
bh-universal: **Prime separation and divisibility vs shear.** Primes cannot be conjugated into each other on one ℓ-adic coordinate (`no-homeomorphism-shifts-odd-denominator-translations`, `no-homeomorphism-shifts-primes-acting-as-l-adic-scalars`). Divisibility on Cantor models is register-type, never group-type, so it cannot be sheared (`w-contains-heisenberg-with-divisible-centre`). GL_n(Q) hosts need primes on separate coordinates, plus a model in which register values can be added.
bh-openq-lists: A regularity class can be a route classifier. PA(S^1), the piecewise real-analytic circle maps, contains T, Lodha's S, Monod's H(R) and every Lodha–Moore group. Yet a closed cycle of four square-conjugations (Higman's H4) has only trivial maps into it, although BS(1,2) itself embeds. So inputs with closed cycles of BS(1,2) conjugations need hosts outside 1-dimensional piecewise-analytic dynamics, and no fp simple group in PA(S^1) is universal. — higman-group-embeds-in-no-lodha-moore-or-monod-group
bh-openq-lists: Mixed identities are the interface between "decidable input" and "fp action image". E(G) ≅ (G*Z)/J_1(G). The image of the BFFHZ relative-automorphism action contains the transvection copy (S*F_{n-1})/J_{n-1}(S) (compare bffhz-q32-forces-finitely-based-mixed-identities). So that universal route needs hosts S whose mixed identities are decidable, which a solvable word problem does not give: fg, and by bh-refute fp, decidable groups can have non-r.e. J_1. — fg-decidable-group-whose-e-is-not-recursively-presented
bh-openq-lists: Simplicity of derived groups of full/rearrangement hosts needs only branching ≥ 3, not ≥ 4. Epstein's trick makes G'/N abelian and generated by finitely many local permutations c. Conjugating by an odd element corrected into G' inverts c modulo N, and the correction error is itself a rigid-stabilizer commutator, so c ∈ N. — dendrite-rearrangement-group-g3-has-simple-commutator-subgroup
bh-refute: Aut_G(G*F_2) for MIF simple G absorbs amalgams along centralizers of finite sets (coset-coded conjugates of P are free), so every centralizer-extension tower (limit groups, ICE, cusp extensions) stays in B_A; template: amalgams over subgroups algebraically definable in the host become permanence theorems — limit-groups-over-hyperbolic-groups-satisfy-boone-higman (verified)
bh-free-12: Equicontinuity principle: synchronous tree dynamics (bounded depth cocycle) and low-dim CAT(0)/hyperbolic-building fixed-point theorems force f.g. torsion to be RF, so by Zelmanov they miss B(2,665) (solvable WP); every BH host must carry NON-RF torsion, i.e. asynchronous or higher-rank dynamics (nV/2-graph vector cocycles, length-changing RSG maps, Kac-Moody with Z^2 ≤ W and a spherical triple) — almost-automorphism-torsion-subgroups-are-rf, hyperbolic-kac-moody-lattices-are-torsion-locally-finite
bh-free-59: FAKE-ACTION TEST. A host ⟨finitary element, dynamical group H⟩ is fp iff R-locally-honest actions are honest for some R. Every sparse periodic H-orbit in a finite quotient carries a locally honest fake (one copy of the finitary element per orbit point), and scaling and local Alt-relations cannot count the copies. So affine/BS-type residually finite thickenings are never fp; Thompson-type fp hosts escape only because small-support elements move one copy at a time. — neumann-doubling-host-is-not-fp (1959141e3)
bh-free-59: A host for any group with finite simple subgroups of unbounded order needs alternating sections of unbounded degree, so no group acting on a bounded-degree rooted tree (no self-similar or automaton host) works; containing A_m for infinitely many m already kills virtual residual solvability. — neumann-group-is-locally-finite-by-z-and-not-fp (35a707c77)
bh-groupoid: Finite-type boundary codings ⇒ B_A. If a group acts properly and cocompactly on a space with a regular "box calculus", its boundary groupoid is Li's groupoid of a finite degree category with finite unit groups (the stabilizers). The transfer is completely reducible for free, by orbit–stabilizer counting, so the only dynamical input is topological freeness. Every cocompact Euclidean-building lattice lands in B_A this way, in any type and dimension; irreducibility and primitivity are red herrings for BH. Limit: these hosts have exponential word problem, so a universal BH host must use a coding that grows with the input. Faithful unit actions on the boundary are residually finite, so the input's complexity must enter through tail-equivalence bisections, not units — euclidean-building-lattices-lie-in-permutational-bh-class
bh-kourovka: Residue-class arithmetic is a one-vertex k-graph with the carry rule (m,r)(m',r')=(mm',r+mr') on prod_p Z_p. So Kohl's CT_P(Z) are fp simple F_inf full groups (Li + AH), and every f.g. group of zero-shift RCWA permutations sits in one. Two multiplicatively independent dilation bases make the host rigid (Cobham forces normalizers to be affine, so Out = C_2); one base (V) stays flexible (Out(V) = O_2) — ct-p-z-is-a-one-vertex-k-graph-full-group, out-ct-p-z-is-c2
bh-kourovka: Torsion local finiteness in k-graph (Thompson-type) hosts depends only on where the degree cocycle lives. If it lies on a ray Zv with v >= 0, then one-stack pumping makes every f.g. periodic subgroup finite, in nV, CT(Z) and any aperiodic k-graph. So infinite periodic/Burnside inputs need a rank >= 2 or mixed-sign (Collatz 3/2-type) cocycle; target those regimes, not the host — periodic-k-graph-subgroups-with-ray-cocycle-are-finite
- [gq-affq, c11fbd4b9 + renormalization-heights-force-eigenvalue-roots] BS(1,m) in full-group hosts = "self-similar time": a bounded local conjugacy from T^m on one tower level to T. Exact m-fold timings, odd and even, are abundant among small reversible machines. The gate is global recurrence: no trapped patterns (a periodic point kills every odometer factor) and bounded glue (no unbounded sweeps). Test in that order. One base carrying several heights must factor onto Ẑ, since E(T) is closed under m-th roots.
- [gq-affq, affine-2v-hosts-reach-z-1-2n-by-doubling-but-not-q; cf. bh-free-30 mass modules, item 9] Prime locality: dilation by p is a bounded digit shift only in base-p coordinates. Roots of order q are bounded only as carries in base-q counters or as finite-state q-automata. A finitely generated host holds finitely many such (coordinate, automaton) pairs, so it sees finitely many primes: 2V_{BS(1,3)} holds Z[1/6] ⋊ <2>, and no affine host holds the natural Q ⋊ <2>. GL_n(Q) hosts must break "one coordinate, one local rule".
bh-houghton-morse: Houghton's n>=3 rule recurs inside near groups. A Z-indexed stack of strands (lines at a critical direction) is an H_2-type object, and if its two ends have equal speed (zero flux) it kills finite presentation of any F_2-free near group. So linear rigid enumerations must transport strands between stacks (nonzero flux, at least 3 non-antipodal stack ends), and Sigma-methods are blind once R_nu contains F_2, which is exactly the open BH regime — zero-flux-strand-stacks-obstruct-fp-amenable-near-groups (05c53d0dc), piecewise-translation-z2-enumerations-are-not-fp
bh-free-22: For annular Thompson-like hosts, short relations = finitely many germ words evaluated at each depth + a finite seed. If the germ group is (input germs) * <zoom>, nothing crosses depths and the host is never fp. Hosts must encode the input in germ relations that cross depths (t^-d u t^d = v) — annular-hosts-with-free-zoom-germs-are-not-finitely-presented
bh-free-11: Finiteness transfer: a monoid with a UFP* degree map to N^k, lcms, finite residues and an F_∞ unit group gives, after × Cuntz, an F_∞ simple highly transitive host (Li thm:deg + Künneth + Li vanishing + Matui). So PBH for G reduces to representing G by boundary germs of such a monoid, and only the unit group needs F_∞. Ax+b monoids of PIDs do all RCWA groups; Hecke branching (failure of UFP* for matrix monoids) is the exact wall for lattice-coset / GL_n-type hosts — pbh-via-degree-monoid-boundary-full-groups
bh-one-relator: Orbit-finite edges are a property of the SPLITTING, not the group. Every A*_φ is also the HNN extension of the double A *_(φC_1) tAt^-1 along the whole vertex group A → tAt^-1. So the stable-permutation engine's forced orbit-finite-edge clause can always be met by making A orbit-finite, and thin or proper-power edges stop being obstructions. HNN permanence thus reduces to a SYMMETRIC actor for the double (the two copies of A must act alike) plus one Britton faithfulness condition. For BG the double is the height-two BS tower, and the symmetry is exactly what finite-entropy Cantor invariants forbid — hnn-extensions-split-over-their-vertex-group-via-the-double (3ee14620b)
bh-one-relator: Prime compatibility. In a compact (equicontinuous) regime, x ~ x^k with p | k forces cl⟨x⟩ to be p-free. Hosts whose section groups are pro-p (binary-tree isometries are pro-2) therefore absorb only power relations prime to p. Test a candidate host by comparing the input's power primes with its sections' pro-structure; on a collision the conjugators need unbounded exponent cocycles or non-isometric maps. Sharp: x ↦ 3x conjugates the odometer to its cube — equicontinuous-square-conjugators-are-trivial-in-brick-hosts (referee-a PASS 9d96cb601)
bh-verify-cbm: A finite kernel of an explicit host is harmless exactly when some finite (or B_A) quotient detects it. For relatively free groups, compute the kernel homologically (Koszul resolution plus coinvariants of exterior powers), then patch it with a nilpotent quotient. The BH frontier this leaves is finite central kernels that die in every finite quotient (Deligne, Q_{g,c}), not representation theory. — free-centre-by-metabelian-groups-are-linear
bh-shell-kazhdan: Pro-p models of shell self-similarity force twists that are unipotent mod p, which kills Anosov/Fibonacci twists (odometer-stable-twists-are-unipotent-mod-two). Any profinite model is residually finite, so it can never reach BH's reduced simple inputs. For simple Kazhdan inputs the window-zero shell needs a non-profinite, highly transitive self-replicating action; otherwise the route must go through windows N ≥ 1.

## bh-free-17 (09-18): germ cocycles make quasi-retractions free; Q1.10 is purely a finiteness question
- **Tool (cocycle quasi-retraction).** Let a finitely generated host act on a Cantor set, let `c(g, ξ) ∈ Λ` be a germ cocycle, and let `ξ_0` be a point. Then `g ↦ c(g, ξ_0)` is a quasi-retraction onto `Λ` if two conditions hold:
  - `c` takes finitely many values on each generator;
  - `c(λ, ξ_0) = λ` for global elements `λ ∈ Λ`.

  Belk–Zaremsky's `SV_G` (twist cocycle) and LISW's `ρ` (linear part) are both instances.
- **Consequence 1 (a partial answer to LISW Q1.10, not a full solve).** Since the host is never more finite than the input, "same finiteness" just means "at least as finite". So Q1.10 is settled for every finitely generated group of type FP_2 over no field, via `SV_H` of the regular action. This includes `Z wr Z ≤ GL_2(C)`, which is not linear over Q-bar. — `non-fp2-groups-are-quasi-retracts-of-simple-groups`
- **Consequence 2 (a test for hosts).** Suppose a host's germ group is realized by global elements. Then the host is no more finite than that group. A piecewise-affine host over a transcendental ring therefore inherits the non-finite presentation of the metabelian group `R ⋊ ⟨p, λ⟩`: valuations trivial on `Q` put an antipodal pair into `Σ^c`.
- **Barrier.** Take a rigid (section-closed) digit host `R^n ⋊ Q ≤ Aff(Z_p)` with a persistent retraction. If it is finitely generated, then `R` is finitely generated, hence algebraic. So transcendence has to live in the host's dynamics (substitutions, or non-residually-finite carries), not in constant linear parts. — `rigid-affine-hosts-are-linear-over-number-fields`
- **Why twisted Brin–Thompson hosts stall above length 1.** On fibres, pairs are counted by the input's own orbits on pairs. So the input itself would need a type (A)-like action.
bh-free-13: A simple host that splits over a subgroup never does so acylindrically: its edge stabilizers are s-normal and of infinite index (Minasyan–Osin). So amalgam/HNN permanence routes to BH must end in Burger–Mozes/Camm-type splittings with fat, commensurated-like edge groups, never small or malnormal ones; it also forces beta_1^(2)=0 once one edge group is f.g. — simple-groups-on-trees-with-fg-edge-groups-have-b1-zero
bh-free-13: Packing principle: a shift permuting a Sidon family of disjoint pieces, plus ONE element spread over the family, recovers every commutator of the pieces. So f.g. simple dynamical hosts (circle groups containing T, vigorous Cantor groups) are 2-generated, generation rank never obstructs BH, and any finite embedding datum placed in one piece packs into a single element of a fixed fp host — fg-simple-circle-groups-with-t-are-two-generated
bh-free-13: Absorption lemma: in a locally moving host, any subgroup containing one rigid stabilizer G_U and closed under infinite-overlap conjugates is everything; so G_U is wq-normal and all cocycle/L2/cost invariants of dynamical BH hosts vanish. Obstructions to BH must be non-L2; properties that propagate along infinite overlaps need checking on one rigid stabilizer only — locally-moving-groups-have-vanishing-first-l2-betti-number
bh-free-08: Mixed identities of a homeomorphism group are an invariant of its closure (word maps are continuous). So Cantor-dense hosts are MIF, and dense tame interval/circle hosts have J_k decided by Tarski. The FFWZ/BFFHZ computability obstruction to BH ⇒ PBH can only come from hosts whose closure is rigid beyond order, never from Thompson-type hosts. — homeomorphism-group-mixed-identities-depend-only-on-closure
bh-free-08: Universal sentences over a whole group die in overgroups; over a cyclic subgroup they survive. A Mihailova core in a mapping torus converts the second kind into the first inside an fp decidable group. So E(G) and mixed-identity data are not BH-hereditary, and no mixed-identity invariant can separate BH. — fp-decidable-group-whose-e-is-not-recursively-presented
bh-free-09: Power-conjugacy is anti-finiteness. If a ~ a^k (|k|≥2) via b, then b strictly enlarges every finite conjugation-invariant set of hyperbolic data of a (F_a ⊊ F_b; b^-1 divides a's levels by k, so bounded bands are backward-invariant). Hosts whose infinite-order elements have finite singular data (Thompson-type attractor–repeller maps) therefore contain no BS(1,k)-cycle, and BG/Higman-type inputs force elements with infinite or scale-free singular sets (odometer or renormalizable cores). This is the dynamical face of exponential distortion — tame-power-conjugators-have-more-hyperbolic-points
bh-free-09: The Leavitt swindle that makes ring hosts universal for linear inputs is blind to homology. Base change from any subring commuting with a (form-compatible) Leavitt pair is zero on H_2/K_2, symplectic or linear. So a Steinberg/ring host can never carry central-extension data (Deligne, Maslov, Euler) as a K_2 class; the centre must be an honest element of E_N, and BH for central extensions does not reduce to BH for the quotient through K-theory — leavitt-swindle-kills-deligne-center-in-ring-induced-hosts
bh-refute: Finitary mixing (G_1×…×G_n)·FSym transfers F_m exactly from finite-set stabilizers (balanced-recoloring-slice complex, contractible as a directed union of convex slices) — a closure operation for type-(A)-style permutational hosts: finiteness is needed only once, in the pieces — finitary-mixing-preserves-type-f-m (verified)

## bh-free-15 (09-18, 81510be26 + dfc27c52f): Garside boundaries — what is free, what is the real gate
- **Effectiveness is free; only flats cost.** For finite-type Artin A, two "chain points" a^∞, b^∞ (Div(a^n) is a chain) already have trivial pointwise stabilizer (gcd distributes; chains have gcd 1), so A ↷ ∂L is faithful, topologically free if irreducible, and A/⟨Δ^c⟩ ↷ ∂L̄ (compact, no global fixed points) is too; A ≤ A/⟨Δ^c⟩ × Z. (Fills a gap: BBCMP's printed Thm 2.4 proof only shows generators act nontrivially.)
- **Singular germs = persistent transducer states = the input's flats.** B_3/Z (hyperbolic mod center) acts on ∂L̄ entirely by prefix replacements (empty singular set). Every rank ≥ 3 type has a singular point (st)^∞ for commuting atoms s,t. So V-type boundary hosts capture exactly the hyperbolic part; flats must go into singular germ groups (BHM) or into extra colours (k-graph coordinates), as in bh-free-36.
- **Li's F_∞ machine needs commuting tail types.** Li's (t<d) says every expansion reproduces its input type. That holds for k-graphs and trivial-type boundaries; it fails for Garside monoids on Ω_∞ (B_3 explicit). There "sw avoids e" becomes "w avoids s^{-1}(s∨e)". Li's ∂Ω of a Garside monoid is the single point Δ^∞, so the group hides in isotropy (dfc27c52f self-refutation). Non-abelian Garside inputs therefore need a germ-extension engine, not Li's grading engine.
bh-free-46: Residual finiteness of A wr_{G/K} G (A abelian ≠ 1) sees exactly profinite closure of K, independent of the membership-decidability BH needs; so every RF host class (self-similar, automaton, Zaremsky's route) covers only closed stabilizers, and non-LERF coset inputs (PSL_2(Z) ≤ PSL_2(Z[1/n])) require non-RF hosts such as clopen-stabilizer full groups — abelian-lamp-wreaths-are-rf-iff-stabilizers-are-closed (6941e6023)
- [bh-free-36, 3be034f09] cubulated-groups-act-by-bisections-of-a-pointed-cube-category (Attempt 1): over a degree monoid with conditional lcms and a finite Garside family, Li's F_∞ theorem needs only the multiplicity condition (t<d) — every basic clopen reappears twice in its own expansion. That condition is the exact combinatorial content of "self-similar with branching ≥ 2" that a general BH host must supply; non-Ore (non-left-reversible) degree monoids like RAAG monoids are where it becomes a real check.
bh-free-23: BBMZ's host class (groups isomorphic to contracting RSGs) is closed under finite-index OVERGROUPS: induce the action to one labelled copy of the address space per coset, and the nucleus stays inside Nuc_H. Q1.1-type questions therefore reduce to finding one good finite-index subgroup, and every closure property of "contracting RSG" is a free Boone–Higman closure property — contracting-rsgs-closed-under-finite-index-overgroups (24e2f0061).
bh-free-23: The irreducible-core hypothesis is purely combinatorial. With no isolated points or empty cones, it is equivalent to the type graph having a UNIQUE recurrent SCC, which is then a sink. Each recurrent SCC carries loxodromics acting as canonical cone shifts. So "does the BBMZ host exist for G itself" becomes "do all recurrent dynamics of G feed into one class" — sft-irreducible-core-iff-unique-recurrent-component (0b2ec9583).
bh-free-23: For groups whose word metric counts separating walls (Coxeter groups, median/cubulated Cayley graphs, surface groups with the 4g-gon generators), horofunction atoms = unbounded cells of finite wall arrangements. CORRECTED (1a5e6ef0c): in surface groups NO cell is a half-plane, because every wall of 𝒲_n is crossed by another wall of 𝒲_n at the corner of its nearest tile. One-endedness = no single-wall gate, which is why cut-vertex and finite-coset gates (bh-free-52) do not transfer directly; a one-ended irreducible-core proof has to handle sector cells bounded by crossing walls — surface-group-atoms-are-wall-arrangement-cells.
- bh-free-extensions [b882ba60a] amir-lazarovich-simple-lattices-have-torsion: finite quotients of one-vertex lattices on T × X (matched pairs z·D = ζ_z(D)·δ_D(z)) are rigid. "φ(D) = φ(D'')" travels along every untwisted letter, so one collapsed pair of seed generators forces the colouring quotient (Z/2)², and FR is decided by a finite orbit closure. Hence simple lattices glued from BMW seeds equal Λ'^+ and inherit its involutions. Finite-cd simple hosts need seeds with no visible finite residual.
- bh-free-extensions [12066f90a] faithful-generalized-bs-witnesses-pass-to-direct-products: the BLIW Theorem C route depends only on the commensurability class. Having a faithful witness is closed under × (glue the two product graphs along one edge; the kernels intersect trivially). Combined with arithmetic witnesses, whole product families enter at once. This complements the commensurator-rigidity obstruction on main.
bh-free-29: Strongly shift-similar groups correspond bijectively to subgroups B <= NSym(N) containing the near shift, via G -> <Germs(G), s̄> and B -> the FSym-preimage of ker(eta|_B). So a shift-similar host is just a near action with an element of index one, and odometer self-similar groups supply that element for free. Finite presentation then lifts through FSym along genuine actions with finitely many orbits on 4-tuples and fg pair stabilizers; this is the shadow of Zaremsky's oligomorphic type (A) criterion, and H_2 shows the orbit condition is needed. The Houghton-like H_n only helps as a device that makes germs realizable on pairs of rays, and fp spectra are multiplicative. What remains in this family is (a) realizability of one-ended germs and (b) a gluing theorem for realizable pieces — odometer-kernel-preimage-is-strongly-shift-similar, fsym-by-odometer-group-on-the-integers-is-fp, houghton-like-fp-spectrum-is-closed-under-multiplication (e41cd1177, bfdd20233)
- bh-typeA-universal: A non-faithful action is a faithful one with invisible coordinates: abstract SV_G = S-colored subgroup of faithful (S⊔G)V_G, so BZ's Stein machinery applies verbatim and SV_G (any action) is fp ⟺ [A_2], F_n ⟺ [A_n]; relative-PBH's fp step restored, and kernel removal (FFWZ Q5.9) is purely about action images — abstract-twisted-bt-type-fn-criterion
- bh-free-19 (fp-simple-real-box-map-groups-are-not-universal-hosts, fp-piecewise-projective-groups-specialize-to-algebraic-data; 2030248b4, c77e9f4da): Real parameters carry no complexity. For an fp group with a tame action (1-D piecewise-projective, or box maps on [0,1)^n as nV), the representation set is semialgebraic over Q, so it specializes to algebraic data, nontrivially on any finite set. Hence solvable WP, and simple subgroups live over number fields in P (dim 1) or EXPTIME (boxes). All finite-dimensional real-geometric hosts are therefore capped and never universal: a BH host must be symbolic and infinite-dimensional (SV_G over infinite S, RN), with the input's algorithm simulated in its combinatorics.
- bh-free-19 (transcendental-slope-thompson-groups-have-hard-word-problems, 5864dcf68): rotation + two bumps ⇒ length-linear commutators read the Sturmian word of the angle. So Thompson-like families D(V_λ) realize every recursive complexity, and BH for them uniformly in λ is at least the Birget root.
bh-free-10: In full Cantor hosts, stabilizer strata of finite subgroups can be ADDED for free (padding: act on a clopen, trivially elsewhere), but no natural host REMOVES them. So the entire finite-edge obstruction for B_A is "remove intermediate strata", i.e. free torsion, which bundle-twisted Brin–Thompson hosts (sections of ∏_U U) would supply. — finite-subgroups-of-full-cantor-groups-conjugacy-criterion (Part 4), b-a-groups-embed-in-full-hosts-with-free-torsion
bh-testcases: Higher-rank BH-via-MIF is the rank-one boundary criterion applied vertex-type by vertex-type: for products of trees and Ã₂ the lattice input is exactly topological freeness on each vertex space at infinity; only quadrangles/hexagons (C̃₂, G̃₂ — e.g. Titz Mite–Witzel) add a genuinely new obstruction, local point/line-domesticity — rank-two-building-non-domesticity-is-vertex-non-domesticity.
bh-synth-lead: Local rigidity principle: fp means the host's global structure is the unique solution of finitely many local rules with no independent far perturbation. Every kill is a non-rigid model; every success is a rigid finite-type coding. General BH = a quantum-rigid free minimal SFT over an fp decidable envelope (Jeandel supplies why decidability is needed), then ring-side K or group-side P2'. — gq-bh-synthesis-master-route (12a123bf1)
bh-free-37: Clopen straightening. In a full clopen-transitive host (CT(Z): pure infiniteness + K_0 = 0), any finite clopen partition can be conjugated onto a box partition. So "box" = "clopen" up to conjugacy: stabilizer realizations (clopen-stabilizer wreaths) may be taken box-adapted. Box-defined element properties are either disguised dynamical invariants or not conjugacy-invariant at all (Kourovka 21.74(a): explicit YES/NO conjugate pair in CT_0(Z) ≅ V) — ct-z-invariant-residue-partitions-are-not-conjugacy-invariant (d4d13957e)
bh-free-37: Shared-colour routing. Full groups of a k-graph on colour sets that share one colour with ≥4 edges generate the full group on the union (≤60 letters per class transposition). Multi-prime fp simple hosts are joins of single-prime hosts, so embeddings can be built prime by prime — ct-p1-and-ct-p2-always-generate-ct-p1-union-p2 (Kourovka 21.75, NO)
bh-free-51: Rank-one hosts (germ extensions of V, or of a normal-form boundary's Thompson group) absorb only virtually central cyclic commuting directions. For a rational boundary action, the singular set is the path space of a finite carry graph, so it is either finite and eventually periodic, or contains a Cantor set. A normal form that reads commuting subgroups in lockstep or in sequence gives each factor a relative phase shift against the other. That shift lands on the Cantor side unless the other factor has only periodic tails: Z corners, or a center written first. So the Garside structures fail for B_n, n≥5. B_3 passes only center-first, and there bh-free-08's Cent_q(a) condition holds with a=2 while BHM (3) fails, so (b) is necessary but not sufficient. The F_2×F_2 in closed Mod(S_g) (no central side) needs a higher-rank host — deligne-forms-are-singular-on-commuting-parabolic-diagonals, b3-center-first-normal-form-passes-the-germ-condition, rational-germ-singular-sets-are-finite-graph-path-spaces (f2fdbf369, 35c7d5435)
- [bh-free-25, 585741f77] affine-self-similar-hosts-are-exactly-global-field-linear: there are three strengths of self-similar host. AFFINE (constant linear part) reaches exactly the global-field-linear groups. FIBERED (linear part polynomial in parameter digits, positive-char-polynomial-affine-groups-are-self-similar) reaches all linear groups. Only NON-ALGEBRAIC sections can reach non-linear or hard inputs (KMS, complexity root). A localized unit torus (KMS tops) blocks fibering by the unit lemma.
- bh-free-32: Finite presentation sees a host's parameters only through a first-order condition, so hosts whose generators are definable in a tame decidable structure (semialgebraic in any dimension with arbitrary reals; ω-automatic on Cantor space with arbitrary addresses: V, nV, RN, germs along any ray) specialize to tame members (algebraic / ultimately periodic) on every finite set; their fg simple subgroups have WP in PSPACE. Tarski ↔ Büchi dictionary. BH/Birget hosts must write the input's algorithm into the combinatorial rule, not into constants — definable-parameter-hosts-cap-simple-subgroups-at-pspace (0343a8459); non-fp deformation test: fp-piecewise-projective-groups-rigidity-criterion.

## bh-free-49 (09-18): faithfulness is free; graph-of-groups inputs are local (10249e417)
- **Tool.** `pbh-amalgams-over-centralizer-separated-edges`: if a B_A group L contains X and Y along C, and some D ≤ L that is a centralizer in a B_A overgroup has X ∩ D = C = Y ∩ D, then X *_C Y ∈ B_A. HNN version: the edge map is conjugation in L. Proof: conjugate Y by the stable letter of the identity-edge HNN L *_D (D × Z), then Britton. For one-sided retracts Y → C, the separator comes free from L × L_X through the retraction.
- **So** BH-type permanence for amalgams and HNN extensions reduces to vertex-faithful images plus centralizer realization of a separator. The images need not be faithful.
- **Configurations are elliptic.** A pair (b, c) with b conjugate to a proper power and b^-1 c b = c^2 fixes a Bass–Serre vertex. So no closure step creates one; it must come from a primitive host (a type (A) actor or a twisted Brin–Thompson group).
- **Realization transfers.** ⟨b⟩ ≤ BS(1,2) has no internal centralizer witness. It inherits one from the PSL_2(Z[1/2]) tree-vertex lamps via BS(1,4) = ⟨a^2, b⟩ (PSL_2(Z) ∩ BS(1,4) = ⟨b⟩) and index-two Kaloujnine–Krasner induction. General move: cut (G, C) out of an arithmetic pair (G', C').
- **Test cases.** H4 and BG share the vertex group G_1 = BS(1,2) *_Z BS(1,2). G_1 ∈ B_A iff some fp twisted Brin–Thompson group has a, b, c with b^a = b^2, c^b = c^2 and c of infinite order. Linear, quasi-linear, metabelian and closure-built groups have none. The canonical candidate is the TWISTED coset lamplighter M_1 = G_1/[N,N]. Its untwisted twin Z wr_(BS(1,2)/⟨b⟩) BS(1,2) is in B_A. The gap is a stabilizer acting on its lamp, by 2^(2^n) on the n-th fibre, which needs infinite-expansion elements.
- bh-synth-reductions: BH (and PBH, just-infinite hosts, isolated hosts, P1/(E)) ⟺ the canonical simple Kazhdan groups H_Γ = EL_3(LC(2^{C(Γ)},F_2) ⋊ Z/2≀C(Γ)) embed; any host class works iff it contains all H_Γ. See decidable-host-classes-reduce-to-canonical-kazhdan-groups (87daac9eb).
- bh-synth-reductions: Kazhdan filter: amenable, Haagerup (V, T, F, CAT(0)-cube proper actors), residually finite (self-similar, linear) and finite-Kazhdan host classes are never universal. Thompson-type dynamics alone cannot prove BH; hosts must be algebraic and Kazhdan-rich. Same node.
- bh-synth-reductions: closure/permanence theorems collapse to their base (finitary-permanence-closures-collapse-boone-higman-to-base), so they calibrate BH and cannot prove it. Stop adding classes; attack the master route.
- bh-synth-reductions: MASTER ROUTE = boone-higman-via-leavitt-units-of-rigid-sft-overgroups: (E) a free minimal quantum-rigid SFT on an fp overgroup [= M2 effective minimal free subshift + M1 minimal simulation over Λ_0×Z²] ∧ (K) K_1 = K_2 = 0 for the Leavitt tensor ∧ the Khanh import. The canonical H_Γ already sit in (R_Γ⊗L)^×. Ranked gates: artifacts/gq-bh-synth-reductions.md §5.
- bh-synth-reductions: work in characteristic 2. The non-fg central scalars Q^× kill char-0 Steinberg quotients (leavitt-scalar-commutators-block-fp-central-quotients); over F_2 they vanish. Route GL_n(Q) through (E) as a subgroup of the acting group, not through R_L.
bh-synth-obstructions: The swarm's host obstructions come from three mechanisms:
- (Z) property (T)/FW subgroups and Baumslag–Solitar root groups lie in the zero set of every stable length (translation, cocycle, commensurated-set, word length), so a host dies exactly when those zero sets are confined;
- (R) finite presentation is open in the space of marked groups, so an fp host must be a rigid point of its local rules (in 1D, periodic, regular or algebraic);
- (H) hereditary approximation.
Rank ≥ 2 hosts already break three walls: Kazhdan (Ã₂), distortion (2V_τ, 3V) and residual finiteness (tree lattices). The wall still standing is complexity. Every fp certificate on main certifies DETERMINISTIC local rules, which cap the word problem at that of the unit/actor groups, and (Z)+(L) pushes Kazhdan inputs into exactly those groups, so BH recurses one level down. The two ways out are fp certificates for non-deterministic finite-type dynamics (2D SFTs, fixed-point tilings; Matui's aperiodic Z² case first) and a hard fp type (A) actor. — zero-set-confinement-unifies-host-obstructions, research/artifacts/gq-bh-synth-obstructions.md (5f923fc28)
bh-refute: Non-MIF fp simple groups acting on a space have local mixed identities (collapse to P^0 near generic points); an overgroup acting on the same space that breaks the order at every scale makes them infinitely based — so relative-automorphism (Theorem E) permanence needs MIF hosts structurally, not as a convenience — thompson-t-mixed-identities-are-not-finitely-normally-generated (verified)
- [bh-free-26] Depth is the complexity resource: finite-depth hosts (V, nV, Leavitt tensor units over F_q or Z[1/M]) are decided by ONE counting quantifier over depth-O(ℓ) test points, so together they share the bound PSPACE and cannot be universal for BH; a proof must supply elements with depth unbounded by word length (self-similar/germ recursion). (`leavitt-tensor-unit-word-problems-have-one-counting-quantifier`)
- [bh-free-26] Char-0 linear hosts dichotomy: a f.g. simple group linear over a Q-algebra with a free multiplicative Z-form reduces injectively mod some p, so it has no square-zero unipotents; Z[1/M]-forms have congruence quotients; Q-algebras containing L_Q(1,2) block fp central quotients. Inputs with standard infinite-order unipotents (GL_n(Q), SL_3(Z) blocks) need non-unipotent realizations or a Q-internal ring. (`integral-counting-group-avoids-leavitt-linear-simple-hosts`)
- [bh-free-26] The Khanh machine, audited at source, is a host theorem with one input: for f.p. central simple F_p-algebras B, (B⊗L_p)^x/F_p^x is f.p. simple once K_*(B⊗L_p)=0, which Ara–Cortiñas give for every regular supercoherent B. So BH for B-linear inputs reduces to finding regular supercoherent f.p. central simple B containing them. (`khanh-ara-cortinas-hypotheses-hold-for-leavitt-tensor-hosts`)
bh-openq-papers: Identity-edge combinations of B_A (coset wreaths A≀_{G/C}G, centralizing HNNs G*_C(C×K), doubles G*_C G, free permutational products) depend only on whether C is a centralizer in some B_A overgroup. That class CR(G) is commensurability-stable: finite-index up in C (lamp pullback), finite-index change of ambient (monomial embedding G ↪ L_K≀S_n, marker (m,1,…,1)), preimages from B_A quotients, intersections, retracts. So virtual retracts are free (limit/surface/virtually free groups), and over F_m×F_n the closure IS PBH for the Goursat quotients (Q_H ∈ B_A ⇒ H ∈ CR ⇒ Q_H solvable WP). What remains hard in combination theorems is TWISTED edge maps — pbh-coset-wreath-subgroups-closure-properties, product-subgroups-lie-in-cr-when-goursat-quotient-in-b-a
bh-free-44: Permutational hosts are geometrically transparent. SV_Γ quasi-retracts onto Γ (Belk–Zaremsky), and BFFHZ's Γ = Aut_V(V*F_m) retracts onto Aut(F_m). So for subgroups of Aut(F_m), "quasi-isometrically into an fp simple group" is exactly undistortion in some Aut(F_m), and the embedding may be chosen freely. The economical routes (Artin, symmetric automorphisms) sit in coindex ≥ 2 free-factor stabilizers, which Handel–Mosher show distorted; loop braid groups are distorted there. Stabilizing by capping holes with handles moves B_n and Mod(S_g^{b≥1}) into a once-punctured mapping class group, a Hamenstädt–Hensel Lipschitz retract. Tool: route inputs through retract chains, not the smallest ambient group — braid-groups-quasi-isometrically-embed-in-fp-simple-groups, loop-braid-groups-are-distorted-in-aut-free-groups (a3d581f86)
- [gq-affq, correction on smart-type-machines-branch-by-every-odd-number] Bounded glue must be tested on the worst configurations (long background blocks), not typical ones. My M_m passed exact hierarchy counts and ~11k random-tape renormalization checks, yet an unbounded sweep (H0 -> (2j,H,pass)) puts it outside the first-return tool. Random tapes and hierarchy-run counts are both blind to sweeps.
bh-free-11: In any degree-monoid (Li thm:deg) host the unit group only contributes a residually finite image: it permutes the finitely many cylinders of each degree. Non-residually-finite symmetry (BS(1,2), V-like) must come from boundary combinatorics such as baker maps. So a BH strategy should split every target into an RF part carried by F_∞ units and a combinatorial part carried by the Cuntz/k-graph structure — pbh-via-degree-monoid-boundary-full-groups (L1)
- bh-typeA-universal: TOOL for any Thompson-like BH envelope: fp needs only (simply connected Stein-type sublevel) + fp vertex stabilizers + f.g. single-split stabilizers + finitely many orbits of intervals with DISCONNECTED open part (commuting-split pairs); FP_n needs stabilizers FP_{n−e} only on homologically essential intervals of degree e. Long-edge stabilizers never matter — interval-spherical-brown-criterion
bh-houghton-presentation: Envelope germ groups are ascending HNN extensions Germs(G)*_c with the shift as stable letter. "Strongly shift-similar" means the index character is two-sided in Σ^1, and Houghton-type thresholds (MZ's n ≥ m+1) are exactly Meinert counts for a one-sided index character. Test the index character's Σ-sidedness first when designing permutation envelopes. — shift-similar-germ-groups-are-ascending-hnn-extensions
bh-refute: G ↪ Aut(K) × G/K for centreless K plus Aut(F_r) ∈ B_A puts every free-by-B_A group (hence every aspherical free-fibre fibration tower over a B_A base) in B_A; for braid-type groups BH reduces to finding such a fibration, linearity-free; the frontier is 2-dimensional fibres (two cone points, D̃_n) — imprimitive-braid-groups-b-e-e-n-lie-in-the-pbh-class (verified)
bh-free-46: Coset-type BH permanence (wreaths over G/C, identity-edge HNNs over C, C as a centralizer) is a finite-index property of (G,C): it lifts from any finite-index H ⊇ C via the Kaloujnine–Krasner cocycle map, so every virtual-retract theorem (Hall, Haglund–Wise quasiconvex, Wilton limit groups) transfers into B_A; its reach is exactly profinitely closed C, complementary to clopen-stabilizer hosts for dense C — pbh-coset-closure-passes-to-finite-index-overgroups (b75ef5e0f)
bh-free-53: Shell germ algebras are never simple, but A/J is always central simple, where J is the singular ideal. J is generated at p by J_p = the annihilator of the Schreier-boundary permutation modules of the germ group. For generic enumerations the germ group is the parity wreath <P,τ | [P, τ^D P τ^-D], D odd> and J_p is NOT finitely generated: the Bieri–Strebel shift exponent D reappears inside the singular ideal, so passing from E_ν to A/J moves the obstruction instead of removing it — generic-shells-have-infinitely-generated-singular-ideals, shell-germ-essential-algebras-are-central-simple
bh-free-02: Host programs are stratified by word-problem class, set by witness depth × evaluation mode: polynomial depth + monomial = coNP (nV, FV over P-coefficients), polynomial depth + mod-2 superposition = coNP^⊕P (Leavitt tensor units), exponential depth + finite-state recursion = PSPACE (V_d over automaton groups). Each program is non-universal above its rung, and the automaton/lift-presented program is exactly the PSPACE rung: it would give a PSPACE-complete fp simple group (via Wächter–Weiß) but never Birget's full question — fp-simple-group-with-pspace-complete-word-problem, automaton-tail-full-groups-have-conp-relative-word-problem
bh-free-02: Lift-presentation (fp of V_d(G), what the automaton route to BH consumes) passes DOWN to invariant sub-alphabets under relator lifting and not up (persistent Grigorchuk): a second retraction test beside loop retraction, and a design rule that overgroup constructions must add relator-killing states, not letters — lift-presentation-passes-to-invariant-subalphabets
bh-free-48: FSym-envelope finite presentation splits into a germ gate (R_nu is f.p.) and a pinning gate (nu is the unique far-out solution of finitely many near relators plus a finite seed, so Cayley(P) ∪ ray must self-assemble by local rules). Generic, free and infinite-index-supported designs die at pinning. Polyhedral one-ended designs pass it in transplant form (the spiral is ball-rigid), so for one-ended inputs the germ gate carries the weight — houghton-like-envelope-fp-pins-the-enumeration-by-near-relators, square-spiral-z2-enumeration-is-ball-rigid
- bh-self-similar [b86b8f18e] nekrashevych-fp-iff-cycle-germ-groups-fp: finite presentation of a Nekrashevych host is LOCAL — for any automaton, fp V_d(G) forces fp germ groups at stable rays; for subexponential activity it is exact (cycling relators = germ-groupoid relators at the finitely many cycle rays). The cycle germ groups sit in (strictly lower-degree automaton group) ≀ finite, so BH-through-self-similar-hosts is an induction on activity degree with a single germ-enlargement step; tree automorphisms cannot realize the prefix shift that V-type germ extensions get for free, which is the whole gap between self-similar and germ-extension hosts.
bh-free-12: (RT) — f.g. torsion in fp simple groups is residually finite — is the uniform Burnside separator (true ⇒ BH false via B(2,665)); proved on every synchronous/low-dim-CAT(0) host family, open exactly on nV, asynchronous RSGs, twisted BT/type-(A) actors, KM with Z^2+spherical triple, SFT full groups; general routes must import torsion via singular germs (shell) or the fp actor (PBH) — fp-simple-groups-have-residually-finite-torsion
bh-free-13: Every known fp simple group (splittings over f.g. groups, locally moving hosts, product lattices, Kazhdan) contains an infinite f.g. ws-normal subgroup of infinite index, which forces beta_1^(2)=0 (Peterson–Thom). An fp simple group without one would refute Osin's problem (fp + beta_1>0 => acyl. hyperbolic) and is the only L2 route to a 3-generator fp simple group: "hyperbolic-like" fp simple hosts are the unexplored frontier — fp-simple-group-with-positive-first-l2-betti-number
- [gq-affq, 4c50f5e9d ballistic-machine-runs-give-periodic-points] Renormalization witnesses from machines must turn on every constant background. One ballistic rule creates a domain-wall periodic point that no inducing can remove (Z_m is torsion-free). It is a one-line table filter to run before any timing or periodicity search, and it explains why sweeping designs pass counts and random tapes but fail globally.
bh-free-06: The finiteness of a Thompson/RN envelope comes from ANY F_n group that maps onto the labels through a recursion whose kernel dies under splitting; the labels' own finiteness is irrelevant. Model non-faithful labels by an EI cloning category with a calculus of fractions, not by a Stein complex: automorphism groups need not inject, and descending links only fatten into polyhedral joins of BK_1 over Cohen–Macaulay matching complexes, which costs no connectivity. Consequences: V_f is F_∞ for hyperbolic PCF rational maps (cover π_1, free); contracting Skipper–Zaremsky reduces to supplying covers. — wreath-recursion-thompson-groups-inherit-fn, contracting-groups-have-dying-kernel-covers-of-type-fn
bh-synth-hosts: Rank one is local. In any full group whose cones form a tree (V, V_{n,r}, graph full groups, RN, Exel–Pardo, and V-germ extensions via the almost-V node), FW rigidity transfixes the cone markings. So a Kazhdan input virtually lives in the local-map or germ groups, and an infinite simple one inside a single local group. Rank-one hosts can only relocate a simple Kazhdan input, never make room for it; reduced BH inputs need non-tree cones (rank ≥ 2) or asynchronous local maps that already contain them — rank-one-cone-hosts-put-fw-groups-in-local-map-groups (03663c211)
bh-synth-hosts: Units are residually finite in every coded groupoid (units permute the finitely many cylinders of each degree), so simple inputs must be non-unit bisections, and a host's word-problem complexity can only come from RF units, germ groups or twists — finitely-coded-unit-groups-act-residually-finitely (03663c211)
bh-synth-hosts: The single missing host engine behind closed MCG (𝒯_m, m≥2) and Deligne (2V_Λ(n), n≥2) is the same: finite presentation for full groups of non-LCM dissection categories, i.e. a Li/Witzel descending-link theorem without lcms (via weak factorization). It is the highest-leverage host theorem on the board — research/artifacts/gq-bh-synth-hosts.md §4
- **bh-free-17, carry lemma (50410e866).** Let a self-similar group have a persistent retraction onto H with carry kernel K.
  - The elements of H that centralize K and fix level 1 embed in K^X through c ↦ (c^x c^{-1})_x.
  - H/C_H(K) acts faithfully on K.
  - So the self-similar machinery creates no complexity. Every quasi-retracting (persistent) host must put the input's wildness into K or Aut(K).
  - Finite-rank carries, as in LISW's Z[1/N]^n ⋊ ⟨N⟩, never reach inputs with infinite-rank abelian subgroups: not Z wr Z, and not Z[t^±,(1+t)^{-1}] ⋊ Z^2.
  - Non-persistent (parameter) hosts escape by absorbing carries into substitutions, at the price of coarse control. — `persistent-hosts-build-the-input-from-its-carries`
- gq-stv-host: Symmetry inside the input is invisible. An acyclic group acting by inner automorphisms splits off (St_N(R_L) ⋊ V ≅ St_N(R_L) × V), and a group containing a quasisimple K normally has only K's simple quotient. So a host must contain the input NON-normally and must give Z(input) infinitely many conjugates; ring or Thompson symmetries fix the scalars and act on K_2 by ±1, so they never do. — leavitt-steinberg-thompson-extensions-add-nothing (095dde713)
- gq-stv-host: Over a Leavitt ring all GL_n(R) are one group R^x ↪ PE_3(R). BH for R-linear groups is then exactly finite presentation of one PE_N(R): it holds for L_F2(1,2), and fails for every R ⊇ Q (rational scalars). The GL_n(Q) obstruction is purely the central Q^x, the scale-shift gate. — leavitt-linear-groups-live-in-one-projective-elementary-group (9583ec1ad)
bh-writeup: Freeness passes germ gates but kills finite presentation in every framework (free-shift Houghton envelopes; G*Z germs of RF groups over V fail SingFix), and fp pins local data (enumerations, slopes, schedules, normalizers) to regular form; so a BH compiler must get its complexity from composing finitely many regular rules (machine-like dynamics, cf. SMART in nV), not from data — free-shift-houghton-like-envelopes-are-not-fp, annular-free-factor-germ-extensions-fail-bhm-singfix, houghton-like-envelope-fp-pins-the-enumeration-by-near-relators (synthesis in gq-bh-bh-writeup-state.md 408c4dc50)
bh-free-58: A fixed fp simple group (CT_P(Z), type F_inf) holds arbitrary reversible Minsky machines as single elements, via the two-involution reversibilization f (+) f^-1 = s2 s1 (buffer copy 2X); element dynamics is undecidable while the word problem is trivial. So BH is not limited by host expressiveness; the task is to turn the computation into identically-holding relations — ct-z-finite-cycle-problems-are-undecidable
- [bh-free-16, c909e7b9e] v-element-invariant-measures-live-on-the-periodic-part: prefix-replacement germs are measure-rigid. The height cocycle (letters written minus read) makes every orbit repeat a cone, contract one, or blow a deep cone onto a shallow one, so atomless invariant mass lives only where the element is periodic. With Markov–Kakutani, a V-element twisting an amenable group acts periodically on an invariant closed set and never dilates a divisible group without fixed points. Host test for Q ⋊ ⟨2⟩, Aff(Q), GL_n(Q): the germ groupoid needs infinite-order bisections preserving atomless mass off their periodic part (odometer or multi-coordinate hyperbolic germs). One-coordinate Thompson germs never qualify.
bh-lattices: Finite boundary codings multiply. Lattices in products get strongly connected orbit categories from factorwise complete reducibility, and effectiveness from a discreteness-free Lemma H (fixing an open set of chambers at infinity forces fixing balls of every radius). So the coded-host machine is closed under lattices in products: every cocompact lattice on thick Euclidean buildings and trees is in B_A. — reducible-euclidean-building-lattices-lie-in-type-a-class, automorphisms-fixing-open-boundary-sets-fix-large-balls
bh-refute: Non-RF in Kazhdan extensions arises exactly where a unipotent completion kills a rational central line: the line survives as a central Z with class a nonzero multiple of the universal-cover class, making the group Deligne-type; so hosts for such families must be the non-RF Deligne-lattice hosts, and the payoff is making that host uniform in the central-extension class — torelli-lower-central-quotients-are-not-residually-finite (verified)
- [bh-free-20, 9bc09ee12] f-infinity-tail-ascending-hnns-satisfy-boone-higman: an ascending HNN extension costs a Thompson-like host ONE new singular orbit. The stable letter is a ray shift in F. The germ group there is an ascending HNN extension of the "tail group" E(G) (image of G along the first sections ψσ^k), which twists against the dilation, so BHM's normal/finite-index germ hypothesis fails (Basilica: Z[1/2]^2 ⋊ Z). BHM's Appendix A induction still gives F_inf: each SingFix group is an iterated ascending HNN extension of Fix(V_{d,m}S) × E(G)^m. So BH hosts absorb ascending HNN extensions with F_inf tail, dilation (BS-type) dynamics included. First use: the Bartholdi–Virág Basilica group (fp amenable, not in SG) is in an fp simple group.
bh-algebra: Gate G3r is exactly one Farrell–Jones instance. For ANY Λ with free finite subgroups, K((LC(X,F_2)⋊Λ)⊗L) = 0 ⟺ FJ assembly for Λ with coefficients LC(X,L) is an equivalence, since the assembly source always vanishes. Putting G inside Λ (S1 ring side) therefore needs a new FJ case for groups containing arbitrary decidable groups. Keep Λ in a fixed FJ class (Z², F₂×F₂, surface or lattice) and load the complexity into the subshift and the unit group — free-crossed-leavitt-k-gate-is-a-farrell-jones-instance
bh-free-42: One-vertex product lattices are transducers (zD = ζ_z(D)·δ_D(z): state z reads D, outputs ζ_z(D), moves to δ_D(z)). Abelian quotients of the colour-preserving subgroup are exactly transducer cocycles f1(D)+f2(ζ_z D)+c = h(z)+h(δ_D z), and a perfect transitive group of legal moves leaves only constants. So NST-simple hosts are never cut down by abelian or solvable quotients: the finite residual is the explicit index-4 colour subgroup unless a NONABELIAN finite simple quotient exists, and one relator r ∈ Γ^(∞) computes it (Radu's device), which gives explicit presentations of such hosts. bh-graph-houghton: BFFHZ's Hom_G action localizes to asymptotically rigid actors: for eventually-rigid G-automorphisms of G*F(X) (Houghton germs), finitely supported homs to G give faithful (MIF), highly transitive, fg-stabilizer actions from the finite-rank lemmas plus slides; graph/surface/braided Houghton groups all reduce to ONE finiteness gate (a bounded-support 'stable' presentation of Aut_G(G*F_n)) — eventually-rigid-g-automorphism-groups-act-with-type-a-except-fp (ebc91762f)
bh-shell-arith: Finite index in a full-group host is LOCAL: a compressible subgroup of a purely infinite minimal full group contains D(F) iff it contains D(F_C) on one clopen C (Matui simple2); compressibility is free from any tree-like piece, and the only obstruction is equicontinuous rigid stabilizers (tree compatibility is one instance). bh-dendrite: Quotient a Stein–Farley complex by each cell's local symmetry (e.g. the ι↔τ flip of an undirected replacement rule) before computing descending links; symmetries the group can undo otherwise create fake empty descending links at every height that mimic an LRP failure. Then tree-shaped rules pass by 'half the vertices of a finite tree have degree ≤ 2' — dendrite-rearrangement-groups-are-finitely-presented (answered in the literature: Perego–Tarocchi 2412.04138; check the question author's later arXiv listing before opening printed-question nodes)
bh-free-44: Every type-(A) closure theorem should be re-audited as a QI closure theorem. Finite extensions turn virtual results into whole groups: residual finiteness plus Birman–Hilden settles closed genus two. - [bh-free-26] G3r ring gate is a Nil question: for ANY algebra C, K_n(C⊗L(1,2)) is the ev_1-image of NK_n(C⊗L) (doubling δ is ×2 on K yet polynomially homotopic to id via an even-permutation unit), so K_1=K_2=0 for (crossed product)⊗L ⇐ K_1,K_2-regularity; KH(C⊗L)=0 always. bh-openq-lists: The whole residually finite sector of BBMZ 5.3 (closed MCG, Out(F_n), fp metabelian, fp RF) is ONE host problem. All fp RF groups are EFRF^+, and Bishop–Schesler Frattini-embed EFRF^+ groups in EFRF^+ spinal branch groups over computable growing alphabets. So a fp simple host construction for that single spinal class settles all four items. Unlike the self-similar route, it cannot die at "some fp RF group acts on no regular tree". — efrf-groups-frattini-embed-in-efrf-branch-groups
bh-refute: One-relator BH is now reduced to the non-ascending core (Magnus–Moldavanskii steps with both edge groups of infinite index, e.g. Baumslag–Gersten); the missing tool is decidable-edge HNN permanence restricted to Magnus subgroups — almost-all-one-relator-groups-satisfy-boone-higman (verified)
bh-free-06: For full-group hosts, the entire 'F_n simple envelope' problem is one gate: an F_n cover of the nucleus data with a dying kernel (orbit/stabilizer clauses via bh-free-45, faithfulness via categories); e.g. IMG(f) ⊂ F_∞ simple SV_{V_f} for hyperbolic PCF rational f — dying-kernel-covers-give-f-n-simple-envelopes
bh-free-59: PERIODIZATION KILLS LINEAR DYNAMICS. For a host N ⋊ Q (N locally finite, generated by Q-translates of one finitary element, RF via finite coordinates), any subgroup O of the limit set that is invariant under Q's linear parts and misses the finite difference sets carries a fake of every truncated presentation (one copy per point of O), on which the finite window groups get infinite orbits. Every affine action over a residually finite ring has one (kA^d), so no affine thickening, over any Z[1/N]^d, is fp. fp RF hosts of this shape need non-linear stabilizer dynamics with no invariant separated sublattice; Houghton-type fp hosts escape because Q is transitive on the limit set. — affine-thickening-hosts-are-never-fp (f3b38ea4e)
bh-garrido-kodama: Analytic hosts are recursion-rigid. In any analytic tree coding, a contracting recursion y∘g_u = g_v∘y pushes every breakpoint of y to the fixed point of g_u, so a self-similar generator's finitary and recursive branches must be analytic continuations of each other. That is why Kodama's G_0(n) (n ≥ 3) has no analytic coding; general BH hosts must be prefix-replacement (Cantor) type — analytic-codings-make-contracting-recursions-analytic, n-adic-lodha-moore-groups-have-no-analytic-tree-coding (fba4edd5f)
bh-garrido-kodama: Build full-group hosts as A(𝒢), not D([[𝒢]]). A(𝒢) is simple for every minimal 𝒢 (Nekrashevych) and equals the subgroup of LOCAL commutators (pairs supported in a clopen with two disjoint translates). So D = A (Garrido–Reid, Matui) is purely a fragmentation question; the host gate is only finite presentation of A(𝒢), automatic when [[𝒢]] is fp and [[𝒢]]/A(𝒢) finite — alternating-group-is-generated-by-local-commutators (fba4edd5f)
bh-one-relator: HNN permanence splits into (i) a CONJUGATING OVERGROUP in B_A, realizing the edge map by a real conjugation (only a quotient of the HNN extension that is faithful on the vertex group is needed), and (ii) identity-edge permanence, which is a lamp-wreath closure. For BG, (i) is forced to contain a Higman-type quotient, so H_4 ∈ B_A plus Z/2 wr over the cosets of ⟨a_0⟩ gives BG ∈ B_A: the two flagship torsion-free test cases are linked, not independent — baumslag-gersten-pbh-reduces-to-higman-group-and-a-lamp-wreath (175da33f5)
- bh-free-32: Specialization has a phase-space twin: full-group elements preserve closed invariant sets, so a dense family of tame invariant pieces (finite orbits → V) makes every simple subgroup tame. Hence master-route SFTs must be free and minimal over a non-virtually-cyclic Λ, and hardness can enter only via WP(Λ₀) through the shift rule or via L(X) — periodic-dense-sft-hosts-have-only-thompson-simple-subgroups (44ba929bc). Twisted BT hosts are exactly as hard as the actor's permutational problems; automatic actors ⇒ PSPACE — automatic-actor-twisted-brin-thompson-groups-have-pspace-wp.
bh-free-22: Positive side of local rigidity: ONE deterministic cross-depth rule makes a Thompson-like host fp.
