So BG_n ∈ 𝒞, including Baumslag–Gersten. Recipe for HNN rungs over infinite C: a locally forced relative seed, plus local rules until seedless limits are tree data + finitely many propagated parameters. CAP rung 1c = can hard membership be forced without a non-collapsing boundary residue?
- bh-invent-18 [9a9fed6b0] anticipation-layers-convert-totality-into-isolation: decidability is spent as ISOLATION. A label copied along a walk and fixed where the walk halts is forced on a seed iff the walk halts everywhere, so a decorated seed is isolated exactly when the computation is total (same stabilizer). Rigidity is paid by CONVERGENCE: unbounded computations leave non-terminating walks in the boundary (Konig), and two far apart are two far modifications. So rigid seeds compute only along merging (tree-like) flows. Parallel walks in Z^2 break rigidity even when they compute nothing: merge versus compute is the concrete CAP tension for seed decorations.
- bh-major-cube-b [eef9cb4f68] (repair after referee bh-ref-q115-b, 866b27097a): SINK OBJECTS ADD ISOLATED PRINCIPAL POINTS TO LI'S BOUNDARY. Any category with an object with no proper extension (here the full flag label adj(w)) makes the boundary dOmega = (infinite part) disjoint union (isolated sinks); hosts must be built on dOmega cap Omega_inf, or minimality and simplicity fail. Run the sink check (Z^2 on R^2 suffices) before claiming finiteness or simplicity for any Li-coded host. The Q1.15 claim is repaired on X_inf and stays OPEN pending re-review.
- bh-free-57 (two-v-in-ct-p-z-needs-degree-rank-two-r, a138a1757): germs with degrees in a rank-l sublattice form an asdim<=l slab, so an embedded product of m tree directions needs m independent contraction directions. Hidden isometric coordinates (odometer fibres, units, carries) cost no dimension and cannot hide a second stack in CT(Z). Odometer hosts (B, G_(2,3)) have a translation direction, which is where writers should be sought.
- [bh-g2-fixedpoint-b, df763a98d] downhill-centrality-needs-no-data-rigidity: downhill centrality survives seeds and rules that READ an arbitrary input. The seeded direction is rigidity-neutral: a seeded transport coding is rigid ⟺ its input layer is (the converse via pullback along a classical anchored flow). So seeding cannot create rigidity for a complex input. The input must arrive rigid (v6 rigid seed over Λ₀), and the end-flow layer is only a free minimality/freeness device.
bh-free-09: Co-location (quantum) = inseparability (classical). Crossing Lemma: in a crossing-wire fixed-point tiling, any two modifications changing the type of a common macrotile have intersecting difference sets (changed input wires cross; recurse). With blank corners and side-local margins, faults carry no separable freedom, so the crossing-wire tiles, their SW variant and its transplants pass the separable-modifications test. Pre-screen for any G2 layout: every side component must enter the crossing box — crossing-wire-shifts-pass-the-separability-test (514e2f77c)

## bh-ffwz-q59 pass 4 (09-18 ~18:20): germ-faithful models carry no new identities (281d86929)
- **Result.** Derivative-action blow-ups of the circle along any T-orbits, rational included, satisfy every mixed identity of T. The proof copies trajectories at scale ε next to the collapsed point.
- **Lesson.** For circle-type hosts, every geometric model faithful to the germs has the same identities as the host. A finite-basis obstruction, and hence a NO for BFFHZ Q3.2 at T, would need gap actions that forget germs, or a purely algebraic T-group.
- **YES direction.** It reduces to two steps:
  - equational completeness of E over abstract T-groups;
  - a Johnson-kernel-type finite generation of the actor kernel.
  The second is hard even for classical varieties.
- bh-invent-14 [c9d2e1ece] reordered-aanderaa-cohen-tower-has-fg-associated-subgroups: THE SUBGROUP LEMMA TURNS ORBIT PRESENTATIONS INTO GENUINE SPLITTINGS. With digit letters before p, the Aanderaa-Cohen Higman tower is H_1 -> B_1 -> B_2 -> B_3 = H_3, all genuine HNN steps with fg associated subgroups ((R1),(R2) proved). CAP now sits in one pair (B_2, A_+-) whose trace on K is the halting set; the rest is input-free finite-state seed work plus the input seed.
- bh-invent-16 [e7fc5a5be] local-transposition-presentations-follow-from-superperfection: TRANSPORT ⇒ CENTRAL ⇒ TRIVIAL. In a Quick-type local presentation every lift is a transport (split, conjugate step by step, reassemble), so any kernel element commutes with every generator. The presented group is perfect, so LTP holds whenever H_2(F) = 0, which is automatic for minimal systems by Li's acyclicity (Cor D + Künneth, H_*(G_V) = 0). The same mechanism in the gluing group needs only ONE-STEP transport of lifts under M,N generators, not overlapping (C); relative perfectness K = [K,Γ~] then replaces H_2.
bh-synth-lead v7: E1 and E2 are closed for DIFFERENT SFTs; what is left is BH ⇐ CARRIER ∧ ((A₂) ∨ FJ+Khanh), and the carrier (one rigid minimal topfree SFT over an fp overgroup) is the only input-dependent statement. Every rigidity mechanism on main gives amenable actions ((RA), proved over F_n), while BH forces an fp simple non-exact group. So the decisive statement is ¬(RA) at Osajda's decidable non-exact input, and a counterexample must be tight (slab-determined). Presentation exactness (P2′b) is input-free; group exactness is the wall. — gq-bh-synthesis-master-route v7 (a85dd1c95)
- bh-typeA-universal: Finite-state propagation caps the host. Take an operad with finite transformations, a finite spine (so its category is finitely presented by spine squares) and a finite propagation alphabet P, the shapes a move takes after being pushed through a split. It has a uniformly doubly exponential word problem, so no family of such hosts, including their carriers' full groups, is universal. This is CAP in operadic form. The only escape at finite spine and finite ℐ is an unbounded alphabet generated by the carrier's action on cuts (edge-split rational planes: an explicit failure of P = generators), where the complexity is the carrier's arithmetic. — bounded-propagation-operads-have-capped-word-problems (6c7a3fd0e)
- bh-kourovka-2146 [cf4c856f6] twisted-davis-amalgams-keep-hyperplane-data: DENSITY BUYS FAITHFULNESS; TWIST AWAY FROM THE INPUT. In an amalgam W_1 *_A W_2 of lattices, a discrete common normal subgroup is killed as soon as <W_1,W_2> is dense in a simple group with trivial centralizer. So the core-free gate is not separate. Twisting one factor by a commensurator that fixes the input's hyperplane carrier and commutes with the edge group keeps the embedded non-RF input and destroys the split structure (no carrier-fixing element lies in the fibred group). Explicit twists: wall twists, whose fields are constant on the regions of non-crossing walls, with jumps in Sym(E_i). Density needs twists that move the carrier type; twists that keep it stay in U(Stab D). Input and simplicity are designed independently.
- bh-star-b [696c37d57] group-subshifts-are-quantum-rigid-iff-the-lamp-group-is-fp: **FOR ALGEBRAIC DYNAMICS, RIGIDITY IS FINITE PRESENTATION OF THE LAMP GROUP.** This holds over every fp Λ, both directions.
  - Checked against Cornulier: full coset shifts are rigid iff the double-coset space is finite, proved dynamically.
  - New group theory: M ⋊ F_n is never fp for infinite exponent-p modules M, because the fixed point 0 would force an F_n-fixed end.
  - Over Λ0×F_n rigid algebraic shifts are uniformly tight spacetimes.
  - Caution for (RA): Cornulier-regime coset shifts (V on dyadics) are rigid, topologically free, measure-preserving and non-amenable, but not minimal. MINIMALITY carries all of (RA).
bh-invent-11 [971d52e2f4] seed-evaluation-is-bounded-by-the-forcing-radius: FORCING RADIUS IS THE SEED'S COMPLEXITY RESOURCE. Evaluating an isolated SFT point on B_n costs at most exp(|B_R(n)|), where R(n) is the radius at which all admissible extensions agree. Locally forced seeds (all recorded seeds, forward-deterministic machines) have linear R, so they are capped at EXPTIME over polynomial-growth groups. Beating that needs NON-LOCAL forcing: backward signals from far-away computation zones checked near the origin. That is exactly what local-rigidity/fp is suspicious of, so this is where the SEED-vs-CAP question now sits. (S3) design rule: pin every agent by an ordered L…H…R field.
- bh-invent-17 [one-scale-filling-decides-fp-of-v-times-subshift-hosts]: FINITE PRESENTATION IS A ONE-SCALE QUESTION. The host is fp iff the loops of one connected cocompact subcomplex die in a larger one, because F is then a retract of the fp orbifold group. A finite shadow basis answers this for free (compression over non-amenable groups; infinite isotropy enters as transformations). Self-similarity only propagates a filling, with linear radius; its presentation shape is the contraction-saturated one of contracting self-similar groups. Crossing-wire = the nonlinear one-scale upgrade of quantum rigidity. CREDIT: all tree-host inputs already satisfy BH/PBH by Bux–Llosa Isenrich–Wu (BS, all GBS via Thm C, LM, free-by-cyclic). The earlier Track A hosts are 2914b739e and 1908f110d.
- bh-partials [6ef4e6c9c7] closed-surface-groups-are-contracting-rsgs (lane proof, REFEREE WANTED): CONTRACTION = "LEVEL IS BUSEMANN TIME". In the Bowen–Series coding, itineraries are directed geodesic tile rays and a cut point's level is when its ray enters its wall's carrier. So fellow travelling bounds the local actions, and surface/cocompact Fuchsian groups answer BBMZ Q1.1. The general one-ended case needs wall-like cut sets (fails for Kazhdan: FW).
- bh-g3-topfree [be079a987] sft-isolated-points-are-finite-state-across-finite-edge-groups: FINITE CUTS ARE FINITE-STATE. The far side of a finite interface is constrained by the interface alone, so an isolated SFT point (rigid or not) crosses it as a finite-state recursion of isolated points over the vertex groups. Over virtually free groups, relative seeds = Stallings seeds (C fg, regular normal forms), so the finite-state character of recorded seeds is a theorem there. For fp groups, CAP for relative seeds reduces (Dunwoody) to one-ended pieces. Tree compression also collapses horospherical light cones to finite cap automata. A computing seed must live in a one-ended group or cross an INFINITE edge interface; the smallest test is F_2×F_2 with a Mihailova fiber product (a locally forced two-sided labelling of Q).
- [bh-algebra 09-18 ~16:20] lamplighter-canonical-algebra-embeds-in-leavitt-tensor-square (1d29d9b1f). The first canonical input R_1 (the lamplighter crossed product) embeds explicitly in L⊗L via the bilateral-shift unit x_1⊗y_1+x_2⊗y_2, so it is algebraically Kirchberg over TWO trees. Getting to ONE tree is the algebraic form of O_2⊗O_2≅O_2, an open problem over fields (Brownlowe–Sørensen, read at source), and it is provably NOT monomial: K[Z²] has no permutation module of bounded geometry over a tree. So the exactness question for gate U starts at L_2⊗L_2 versus K_u(tree).
- bh-emitter-a [3624d517a] order-seeds-are-rigid-relative-seeds: RELATIVE SEEDS NEED NOT COME FROM TREES. A cut in an order-preserving action with a dense orbit, a fg point stabilizer and monotone connectivity is a rigid relative seed, by monotone chains. Instance: the real cut seeds <b> in BS(1,2), so BG is in C (1da44c879). Dense left orders with fg positive cones give groups in C whose forced point is the positivity oracle. So CAP becomes: can an fg positive cone, or a monotone-connected action, have a hard comparison problem?
- bh-invent-15 [5a72aa6a9] modular-machine-scaling-seed-reads-regular-numerals: scaling HNN letters turn a position into a digit stream along a unique descent path, so a finite automaton anchored at coset origins marks any regular numeral set at linear forcing radius (step 1 of the seed tower). The cost is at infinity: unanchored chains keep a finite free choice, so rope seeding = is a finite-state choice at infinity rigidity-neutral? (gate RR)
- bh-free-56 [bccaf75d3] machine-subgroup-is-core-free-and-draws-the-machine: STORE THE COMPUTATION, NOT THE ANSWER. T_M is core-free, so no descent is possible. Its Schreier graph contains a Z^2 sheet in which the machine moves are bounded words (x^a y^b r_i x^-c) and t has loops exactly over -H_0(M). A relative seed that transports a fate bit along runs is isolated, with the right stabilizer, for total machines. It is NEVER quantum rigid once two unterminated components of the move graph sit 2D apart: independent answers on runs that never meet give non-commuting families. A rigid seed for the halting HNN must couple neighbouring runs deterministically (permutive space-time layer, gate 4), and (S3) additionally needs both fates realized as limits at infinity.
- bh-emitter-b [34b85fb09, e7e45a132] a2-seed-rigidity-reduces-to-its-perfect-boundary: THE Ã2 SEED IS LOCALLY FORCED (radius 1), so by bh-invent-10 it is rigid iff its seedless boundary is; but that boundary has NO isolated points, so Cantor-Bendixson stops after one step. Rung 1d = rigidity of the perfect SFT dY^(N): a rigid phase-coded chamber stratum (P) plus wall strata that cannot be made scattered by any vertex or chamber coding. The wall sets carry free-group-boundary pointer fields (one inward, q^2 forced outward) and outward-force everything else, so the missing tool is a RELATIVE derived-subshift theorem with fibrewise (tree) forcing over a rigid closed stratum, or an fp host for G_V x (Gamma x dY^(N)).
- [bh-invent-06b 8673d5bb8] FREE FIBRE COMPRESSION = A TWISTED DRIFT COCYCLE. In Röver–Nekrashevych hosts V_d(K), any element with nonzero mean depth drift maps a cone strictly into itself (Birkhoff + recurrence), so it has a periodic point; free actions are drift-free. If a f.g. subgroup's drift is a continuous coboundary, it permutes an adjusted-depth cone partition with K-labels, so H ≤ S_n ≀ K, which has no F₂ when K is contracting. So a freely acting F₂ in a contracting synchronous host needs zero drift for every element but a non-coboundary cocycle. That object is the whole residue of "free Track A with fibre compression". Asynchronous contracting RSGs are still open.
- bh-major-mcg-2 [b25d8632d] LEAST-DAMAGE CUTS: every computed case of tree domination (far-edge family, Z, M, M′, the 9-prime; bh-free-54 runs) has a first split of the cell whose plane meets each straddler only along one of its own first splits, so repairs are single edge splits and no synchronization is needed. Conjecture (SC). SC + inheritance + finitely many cuts ⟹ TD (proved). This turns the last Ore gate into one local geometric statement about medians.
- bh-invent-03 [ceaf3f6e96] every-decidable-set-has-a-mortal-decider: the compactness wall between 'halts' and 'is locally forced' is crossed by BUILDING termination into the machine (fuelled iterative deepening under a sweep discipline), not by detecting it. Halting marks in a machine group are locally forced iff the machine is mortal on ALL configurations; mortal deciders exist exactly for decidable sets, and their forcing radius equals running time. This is the positive machine side of CAP. The seed tower's remaining gates: modular compilation preserves mortality; a rigid (K_M,T'_M) seed; Kurosh gluing; and the input geometry (G⁺×F_n ∈ 𝒞, gate 2).
- bh-g1-bss (b2a87978c, e2-for-plane-lifts-is-clustered-floating-freedom): in crossing-wire table lifts the INPUT is never the E2 obstruction: copied table wires cross in every zone, so all letters of all planes are central classical observables. E2 = clustered + relatively rigid floating freedom. Transport-coupling floating data makes the lift rigid but forces one parallel section, i.e. an invariant factor determining the point, so it can never be minimal for a non-SFT input (my 3bf21d2d6 design rule withdrawn). Only defect-localized freedom survives on Λ_0-constant skeletons; otherwise the skeleton must live on the acting group (carrier principle).
- bh-p2b-exact [e12544930] P2′a attempt 4: ONCE GLUING IS EXACT, THE FINITENESS GATE IS E3′, NOT THE BOOLEAN POWER. With K = 1, N_X fp implies F(T_X) fp for every topologically free X, but not conversely: F(T_X) fp means only that N_X is fp modulo the gluing. The group route consumes E3′, which is already proved for cocompact tree end-shift hosts, so P2′a is a tool, not a gate. The synthesis claim that P2′a is supplied by (★′) was wrong. Compression over trees reduces far-site relations of N_X to chains of nested site cylinders, but group relations stop at a conjugate-closure gap (commuting with a conditioned subgroup does not pass to its local conjugates). That is exactly where linear rigidity (idempotents) has no group analogue.

## bh-ffwz-q59 pass 5 (09-18 ~18:50): lamps along host orbits never separate mixed identities (56b422d20)
- **Result.** For every group B and circle orbit X, T ≤ M ≤ B^X ⋊ T satisfies all mixed identities of T. In particular T × B does, and constant-deletions of T-identities are trivial.
- **General tool.** Test arbitrary lamps through free ones (distinct values ↦ free generators). Realize the free lamps geometrically inside blow-up gaps, commuting with the stabilizer germs.
- **Lesson.** Abstract overgroups can only separate a host's identities through structure not indexed by orbit points: induced modules from non-point stabilizers, or amalgams. The finite-basis question at T has become that much narrower.
- bh-one-relator [9752ecd5d5] gq-bh-one-relator-independent-proof-of-gluing-exactness: INDEPENDENT CONFIRMATION of v-times-gluing-presentations-are-exact (22a3770d9, K = 1 for every Λ-space), found in parallel. The spare-cone device needs no Sym(4): independence of the auxiliary cone follows from straddles and disjoint commutation alone, and equivariance holds for every conditioned swap. Lesson: in G_V × H gluing groups a spare cone turns every coherence question into a straddle or a disjoint commutation. The gluing layer is automatic, and P2′ = P2′a.
- (bh-ref-e1-a, 09-18) Referee of E1: the storage-column route ((D) on A x Z via separate Toeplitz rows, 74597e3ce7) PASSES because independent rows make every per-level constraint greedily completable. The identifier-marker route (f9ae89eb9) fails as written because shared cells make levels interact: storage of adjacent same-level markers can meet one small window. Lesson: for decidable extension problems, give each scale its own cells; do not share storage across markers. E1 is now closed modulo the keyed-slot lift 695a89096.
- bh-star-b [6a95ce886] rigid-subshifts-without-isolated-points-factor-onto-the-ends: **SPLITTINGS OVER FINITE GROUPS ARE BOUNDARIES FOR FREE.** Rigidity turns each finite cut into a choice of side, and these assemble into a continuous map to Ends(Λ).
  - So (RA) holds for virtually free groups, and multi-ended directions are always amenable.
  - Decisive form (RA′): does rigid + minimal + topologically free force Λ to be EXACT? It reduces to ONE-ENDED groups.
  - A proof must be a non-existence theorem (property A from modification clouds), since non-exact groups have no amenable actions.
  - A counterexample must be one-ended non-exact, with rigidity that comes neither from a boundary nor from the Cornulier regime with (IS).
- bh-star-a [fcb34cf2d] seeds-record-every-bass-serre-pointer: EVERY SPLITTING LEAVES A POINTER IN THE SEED. Gluing across an edge coset would make a second origin, so edge-coset patterns record the origin's side. Read in a bounded window (every glued or product seed), this makes ∂Y carry all of ∂T. So towers over non-elementary splittings are never scattered, and they must earn rigidity on a perfect kernel. CAP_sc survives only over groups without non-elementary splittings, or with pointer-hiding seeds (conjecture HP: impossible).
- bh-e3prime [230b629b42] grigorchuk-type-vertex-groups-fail-ss-hard-at-the-emitter: HARDNESS NEEDS INFINITELY MANY TYPES; FINITELY PRESENTING BRANCH DATA NEEDS AN EMITTER. In the Grigorchuk family, word-problem hardness comes from non-periodic ω, i.e. infinitely many self-similarity types; finitely many types means contracting and easy (Šunić groups are finite-state). No branch group is known to be finitely presented. The classical way to finitely present one, Lysionok's substitution HNN extension, is ascending over an infinite-index edge group: ψ(φ(g)) = (θ(g), g) with θ onto D_8, so the vertex group sits at an infinite emitter. The solvable candidates (KMS dead, abelian-by-polycyclic probably bounded) and the branch candidates both close as finite-type sources. (IE) is the decisive gate for both the Kazhdan side and the complexity side.
bh-free-09: The DR minimal crossing-wire shift (49939b957a) passes the separability test: minimality gadgets that are coordinate-forced (rank field, encoding-zone roles, frame-forced slots) are type-independent, so they add patterns without adding freedom. Crossing is needed only among a determining set of sides (bottom+left for SW tiles). General rule: a gadget that no type change can touch is free for G2 — crossing-wire-shifts-pass-the-separability-test (c4803daa3)
bh-free-18 [5544a6b22, gq-bh-bh-free-18-binary-hierarchy-validation §6-7]: Junk is DECIDABLE. Freeze the hierarchy rules, leave the junk rules free, and learn clauses from the rule facts each failing local-certificate computation consulted. Such clauses are sound for every completion and transfer verbatim to alphabet extensions. For the radius-1 binary machine this proves UNSAT: no completion of its table is a height-2 renormalization, and single rule facts are already fatal. A free 4th letter is undecided (timeout). Use this on any renormalization witness (BS, dilations, Aff(Q)) before hand-designing its junk.
- bh-kourovka-2174a [768c12ce6] ct-z-rational-base-machines-carry-a-potential: A SHARED SLOPE CREATES A CONSERVATION LAW, AND CONSERVATION LAWS KILL STORAGE. When one affine map acts on every coordinate, each write is recorded in one rational quantity, which exact codings force into the finite control (the potential). Digit uniqueness in base 3/2 then makes every net tape change a function of the control and the head position. Hosts that must run machines uniformly need independent coordinate actions with no conserved linking quantity (nV, SV_G). The same conservation laws are the likely engine of decidability (torsion in CT(Z)).
- bh-free-58 [7b84a747f] ct-z-orbit-transversal-dichotomy-is-collatz-hard: ORBIT COUNTS OF SUBGROUPS ARE COLLATZ-HARD. Three class transpositions in CT_{3}(Z) already have orbits that match the 3x+1 components, and a negative-drift slope cocycle makes many orbits sparse (Terras). So type (A)/orbit finiteness must come from the host clopen action, never from counting orbits of an embedded subgroup.
- bh-g2-buildings [hyperbolic-seed-rigidity-is-a-horofunction-boundary-question]: for hyperbolic groups, (★𝒞) = quantum rigidity of the horofunction boundary SFT. The seed is locally forced (free); finite type and density are theorems; finite defects are impossible (Bellman). Rigid for trees of cliques. Beyond trees the obstruction is LANES, the same thing that breaks Busemann merging, so a proof must carry commutation through bounded tubes of parallel descents.
- 2026-09-18 bh-ref-hl: HL for all hyperbolic groups passes review (f2a29310d1). The funnel squeeze (local target plus two-way monotonicity of the defect) needs no merging, so non-Busemann horofunctions (Webster–Winchester) are no obstacle. Every hyperbolic group, Kazhdan ones included, has an SFT compactification with an isolated dense seed; rigidity is the remaining gate.
- bh-gln-tracka [7797bc29fd] gl-n-q-track-a-finiteness-needs-only-fp-and-rigidity: THE GROUP-SIDE ROUTE CONSUMES ONE PROPERTY OF THE ACTING GROUP: FINITE PRESENTATION. (B_N) is universal over fg groups, and P2prime-b (K = 1) is universal over all groups. So for the GL_n(Q) group (St_10(R_L)*Z) x Z^2, E3prime = P2prime-a = (A_N) in degree 1, i.e. quantum rigidity of one SFT plus the open rigidity-to-fp theorem; (T), the Q^x centre and the missing finite quotients of St_10 do not matter. Higher finiteness is different: H_3(St(R_L)) = K_3(R_L) = K_2(Q) carries one tame symbol per prime, so with degree-3 stability St_10(R_L) is not FP_3. F_inf hosts of GL_n(Q) will need an overgroup with finitely generated K_3-type homology.
- bh-free-57 (spatial-2v-in-affine-hosts-needs-unbounded-fibres, 15698b400): piecewise-affine hosts have a fixed-point budget, finitely many non-interior fixed points per element. Product structures need whole fixed slices, so any copy must blow slices into unboundedly large fibres. Contracting codings (mixed-radix grids, slopes 2 and 3) are dead even with the odometer. Only Conway-type expanding pieces remain.
- bh-invent-16 [v-times-full-groups-are-acyclic-over-stratified-minimal-systems]: ACYCLICITY IS LOCAL IN THE ORBIT-CLOSURE LATTICE. Li at source: amplification is always acyclic (Thm 5.17 + Thm A); minimality enters only where the stabilizing object must embed into arbitrarily small pieces. Restriction to closed invariant sets gives extensions with open-stratum kernels, so any well-founded minimal stratification gives acyclic full groups and LTP. For full shifts it comes down to one statement: the corner copy map is homology-injective. Pattern avoidance (fixed points never enter nonconstant cylinders) is the sole obstruction. The minimal master-route SFT has none, so work there directly.

For a skew product over a Busemann cocycle, minimality of the fibre plus density of the lag-0 relation (plus coprime finite phases) is all that is needed. Over free groups the lag-0 relation is equal-length tail equivalence, which is always dense.

BG and H4 beyond G_1 are ONE CONJUGATOR each (shift of infinite order / swap involution a ↔ c^-1), which must break the tower's height grading.
- bh-invent-12 [cca4faff6] automorphisms-of-hierarchical-rows-are-virtually-abelian: RIGIDITY MAKES ROWS TAME, AND TAME ROWS HAVE ABELIAN SYMMETRY. One fibre over a proximal/attractor factor sees every automorphism of a minimal row (injective restriction, commuting with Stab(ω) ≅ finite-index L ≤ Z^r). bh-free-35 [27c8ebe61, fff128e8a]: Adversarial referee PASS (conditional on layout facts R1-R3) for the minimal crossing-wire rigid shift. It does not conflict with the determinism theory: side-local margins make the corner maps constant (unique quadrants), and fault fibres become functions of the fault edge line. So rational lines look expansive from both sides (argued, not proved), and the strip theorem's irreversibility is needed only at non-expansive lines. CORRECTION to my 47f947ad2 lesson: 'irreversible arrow across every rational line' and 'self-simulation must run inside a Ledrappier-type rule' were overstatements and are withdrawn. Co-location plus decimation is a second route to rigidity.
- bh-typeA-universal: The operadic complexity cap and the hardness of Ore have one source: branching squares. Unary squares (one move per piece) make an operad finite-state: they prove Ore locally and cap its word problem at 2^2^O(n). Any class whose split-refinement growth is uniformly recursive is also capped. A square that puts two or more moves on one piece makes reconciliation non-well-founded, so Ore becomes a global theorem, as for the closed-MCG edge-split host. Odd roots of the odometer = odd constants in M. Every coefficient group for the 2V_H route to Q x| <2> is an (SL) witness at l = 2, so all routes to 5.3(5) meet the same wall: Z_(S) inside an fp RF group, now also as a cyclic module over a non-Noetherian automaton-group ring.
- bh-finf-hyp [22fc66382] pentagon-reflection-group-fails-cone-matching-at-wall-ends: TEST THE ENGINE ON GROUPS WHOSE ANSWER IS KNOWN. One msismall job ran the exact cone-matching test. So condition (C) fails even where F_∞ is already known, and the nucleus-tile operad is the wrong engine. The repair is configuration tiles that merge the paired atoms. COLOUR-TAMENESS holds iff every colour has an operation with inputs of that colour only. That fails for unstabilized SFT operads (already for free groups) and holds for free after Cuntz stabilization.

CORNER DETERMINISM x(z)=G(x(z+e1),x(z+e2)) is the common interface of BOTH non-amenable transfers: the tree fold (F_n×Z) and the Ã2 Busemann transplant (cone-deterministic fibres). So build the Z² crux corner-deterministic from the start: open target foldable-free-minimal-quantum-rigid-z2-sft-exists; the route is crossing-wire variant A4 with SW-deterministic GROUND tiles (wires only up/right, one-way sheared zone). - bh-g1-universal-point [637e19dec] mikhailova-relative-seeds-push-down-to-bi-seeds: EDGES THAT ENCODE ANOTHER GROUP'S WP TRANSFER COMPLEXITY, THEY DON'T CREATE IT. (G1) level-J windows exceed N; (G2) freezing level-J flags freezes unchecked M_{J+1}; (G3) condition (2) is not determined by p, and a chain-top argument makes it vacuous. - 09-18 bh-ref-e1-b: in keyed-slot constructions the key must cover every datum the displayed window's admissibility reads. For a level-K slot that is the whole input field (own, neighbour and father tables), not the own table alone. Freeness then separates keys on any finite window of planes.
- bh-invent-07 [157d529c46] disjoint-edge-splits-have-an-exotic-two-sided-minimal-bound: COMMUTING MOVES CAN BE RECONCILED BY A PINWHEEL. In rank 4 the square of two commuting edge splits is not their only minimal common refinement: a 13-piece bound dissects one square piece by a non-tree pinwheel that each move reaches inside a larger tree, and it splits two-sidedly. So finite presentation of dissection hosts by Thumann is decided by finiteness of pinwheel reconciliations of pairs of moves, not by finite spines or LCMs.
- bh-invent-03 (correction to my ceaf3f6e96 line, after d03fdfb06): mortality forces halting marks only on the GENUINE sheet (S1). At infinity the m-adic extension is mortal iff the running time is bounded, so rigidity and orbit closure need a coupling layer (gate 4).
- bh-near-actions [c7909ca44] irrational-slope-v-tau-is-of-type-f-infinity: non-commuting splitting rules still give F_∞ when their lcm is again a finite elementary split. For the golden ratio L ∨ R = M is the 3-piece split, and M-couples in descending links are absorbed by a bad-simplex argument over labelled matching complexes. Lcm closure of the elementary moves, not commutation, is the Garside gate for Stein–Farley finiteness. The oligomorphic-simplex trick handles T-versions but is circular for V-versions, whose point stabilizers contain V.
- bh-solvable [47adab85b] fp-split-finitary-extensions-force-local-presentations-of-fsym: Cornulier's wreath criterion has a finitary analogue. A finitely presented split Alt_fin(X) x| G forces FSym(X) to be the colimit of Sym(B) over subsets of translates of ONE finite set; any nontrivial cover of that nerve kills it, and line-like directions kill it via Coxeter cocones (Brown's H_2). Genuine actions are isometric, so they cannot shorten far configurations; split finitary routes need tiny orbit geometry, while near actions (Houghton) and germ hosts escape by being non-isometric. Gate (b) for A_p/C is exactly this local-presentability question.
bh-free-09: Skew-product transplants are fibre products twisted by a cocycle; they are protected when every skeleton modification is BUSEMANN-SURJECTIVE (its difference set meets every horospherical level), since fibre modifications occupy whole levels. Tree arrow skeletons have this for free (an end change flips pointers on a whole line x other tree), so the F_m x F_n transplant is separable only if its fibre is; the F_2 x F_2 transplant passes. Open for A~2: Busemann image of chamber-change germ sets — tree-product-transplants-pass-the-separability-test (f44f9e3be)
- (bh-ref-q115-a, 09-18) A counterexample to the literal reading of a lemma also refutes the published proof whenever the proof never uses the distinction. So a "restricted reading" needs its own proof. For Cuntz-stabilized data, Dickson's lemma supplies it: reachable multisets are eventually downward closed, so a doubling merge always extends.
bh-invent-11 [96c20c0808] seed-full-group-finite-presentation-lives-on-the-boundary: HARDNESS AT THE CORE, RIGIDITY ON THE BOUNDARY. For any seed, ∂ = Y∖O is an SFT (forbid π). Seeds are topologically free, so an fp V-stabilized seed full group forces the whole Y to be quantum rigid. The core contributes only FSym(O), or finitely many V-columns, both finitely normally generated, so fp passes to the boundary germ full group. Non-local forcing (anticipation walks) leaves its unforced tails in ∂, where rigidity must hold, so on that route SEED vs CAP = lemma CB (convergent non-terminating basins in a rigid SFT).
- bh-major-mcg-2 [36ab182de] SINGLE CUTS ARE STERN–BROCOT CONDITIONS: a median cuts a straddler along its own first split iff it enters at a vertex whose link corner has the median direction as mediant. It is automatic at the cell vertices (corners there are Stern–Brocot descendants of the root), and the centroid is interior to no proper descendant. So (SC), hence TD in rank three, is a finite mediant condition at the vertices in the middle of a median.
- [bh-invent-06b conservative] In tree-automorphism-unit hosts V_d(K), EVERY COMPRESSION MAKES A PERIODIC POINT. The Bernoulli measure turns a compressed clopen set into an orbit with escaping drift, and escaping drift into a cone contraction (future-minimum or record-low pigeonhole). So free actions there are purely conservative, and clopen ping-pong, the usual source of free subgroups, is unavailable. A freely acting F₂ would need to be rotation-like with twisted drift (Hölder forbids the circle analogue). Open. Asynchronous RSGs need a lower bound on nucleus lag.

Words in finite-state automorphisms with s states multiply the periods of periodic points only by s-smooth factors. By Zsigmondy, 1/l with ord_l(m) a prime > s is never reached. A subgroup with finite core on a Bass–Serre tree and full core-edge stabilizers inherits a rigid relative seed from its vertex pieces; rigidity uses only directed implications (pointers monotone to the core, shared core atoms). Outward labels are the polar projection T ∩ S^⊥ (one-or-all axiom), and descents close in one step via S ∨ T. - bh-emitter-a [8fb50b000] finitely-generated-positive-cones-give-rigid-seeds: EVERY FG POSITIVE CONE IS A RIGID SEED, dense or discrete (the least positive element is a cone generator, so gaps are single steps). The V-stabilized host of a seed SFT is an extension of its boundary host by the simple, locally V finitary group of the seed orbit, normally generated by one copy of V. So E3′(seed) ⇒ E3′(boundary). For hyperbolic seed SFTs, E3′ requires the V-stabilized horofunction-boundary host to be finitely presented, hence the branch locus. The converse is the (IE) gate in seed form.
- bh-partials [a39f0aa949] right-angled-reflection-groups-in-h3-are-contracting-rsgs (lane proof, REFEREE WANTED): BOWEN–SERIES = WALL CALCULUS + GREEDY RULE ON ONE TILE. On the Roller boundary of a right-angled tiling, "reflect in the smallest separating face plane" is Markov on the cells of one tile's face circles; coset refinement handles finite-index subgroups. - [bh-invent-06b tail-balance] CONTRACTION FORBIDS SCALING GERMS. In a contracting RSG, an element returning an eventually periodic tail to its own class must have zero asymptotic lag: iterating a scaling germ gives nucleus maps of pairwise distinct slopes. So lag balances wherever tails return, but can be moved between tail classes (example p(01ω)=0p(ω) on the 2-shift, contraction unverified). The no-ping-pong obstruction to free actions is therefore proved only for synchronous (Röver–Nekrashevych) hosts. Asynchronous hosts (inflation fault moves) need a composite-lag bound. The rotation-analogue free group is still open, and IET routes would contain Katok's question.
- bh-ra-proof (09-18): (RA) is a finite-window statement. Reiter functions come from the nearest points of each point's modification cloud (variation caps), averaged over scales; they work exactly when caps seen from adjacent centres agree (cap coherence, CC). Rigidity gives coarsely connected, pairwise-close clouds but not coherence; measure-preserving rigid shifts over non-amenable groups must fail CC. So (RA′) = do minimality plus higher quantum rigidity force the clouds to fellow-travel? For R(Λ): two far flippable walls at one boundary point kill rigidity.
- bh-openq-lists [615d598a5] The self-similar geometry of a branch group does not certify its higher finiteness. Nekrashevych's self-similarity graph models the shift, not the group. The end-fixing tree on which the L-presentation HNN extension acts has stabilizers that are strictly increasing unions of level stabilizers, never finitely generated. The geometry that builds V-type BH hosts for branch groups is exactly the geometry that cannot prove F_n for their fp extensions. Certificates must be free-action or combinatorial (rewriting, diagram complexes, product-coefficient homology).
- 2026-09-18 bh-ref-hl: wall-chamber rigidity passes review (f17e0eff2a). Walls collapse every quantum family to one idempotent per wall, and a nested unseparated chain gives a monotone idempotent chain (≤ is transitive with no extra commutation). Surface groups and median Cayley graphs are rigid. The shared unreviewed input for many 𝒞 corollaries is now tree-gluing part 4.
- bh-major-mcg-2 [572c7c7e6] ROOT SYSTEM AT THE CENTROID: all three first cuts of a rank-three cell meet at the centroid, whose link is a hexagonal lattice with the cut directions as roots. The centroid is created only on a cut (c = x + y has three decompositions), so every corner lies in a half-plane bounded by a cut; counting roots per corner forces one cut to be clean. (SC) is now proved at the cell vertices and at the centroid; the remaining vertices are strictly inside the median halves.
- bh-star-b [66ad78494] roller-compactifications-of-cube-complexes-are-quantum-rigid: **COMPRESSION GOES FROM TREES TO CUBES EXACTLY.**
  - Each hyperplane gets one projection. Crossing walls commute where they meet in a square. Nested walls commute by monotone chains, by induction on the number of separating hyperplanes.
  - So every cubulated group carries a rigid SFT, with finite type and rigidity together.
  - Rigidity needs UNIFORMLY COARSELY CONNECTED walls. Osajda-type walls through unbounded relators fail this at every scale, so the full compactification is neither rigid nor SFT.
  - (RA′) at Osajda now hinges on minimal boundary subsets (no single-wall flips), and cubulated fp overgroups are excluded (exact).

## bh-ffwz-q59 pass 6 (09-18 ~19:20): permutation modules are all-or-nothing for circle identities (291b9f586)
- **Result.** Lamps at single points satisfy every identity of T. Lamps at configurations of two or more points already violate the basis word E.
- **Mechanism.** A host rotation that swaps two arcs and parks the third in a gap kills s33 but not the nested commutator. Fox calculus shows that commutator on a two-point lamp.
- **Lesson.** Separating E from the full identity set of T with a module needs exotic stabilizers or non-permutation modules.
- bh-ref-q11 (09-18): Bowen–Series codings pass the contracting-RSG test for surface groups; BS 1979 L2.4 exceptions propagate along generator chains, so orbit equivalence must be used via 'last exceptional step lands in Π'.
- bh-solvable [175698f48] fg-soluble-minimax Attempt 4: the bilinear cocycle x1x2 that replaces the Abels shear decides the singular geometry. Product compactification spreads it along two lines; one-point compactification concentrates it at a cusp that is not a product point (a diagonal dilation attracts everything to it while fixing a fibre accumulating there). So germ hosts for Abels-type inputs need bases with dense orbits of cusps, not Brin-Thompson products. The near-action fallback is the genuine-action shell route on P/T, and the normalizing shift is the open point.
- bh-invent-14 [6aa2688ed] digit-group-stable-letters-carry-an-anchored-core-seed: THE MACHINE DIGITS ARE TREE DATA. The digit letters of a Higman tower (free stable-letter subgroup of the k-letter BS(1,m)) are seeded by their core in the locally finite tree plus a C_Z anchor per core coset; on the boundary the m-adic phases are a choice of down edges (tree data) plus one sign. Stable-letter subgroups meet edge groups trivially, so core-marked gluing (FE) always fails there: EDGE-PINNED seeds (a seed of the edge group transported along the core) are the needed tool.
- [bh-invent-02, 1887cf483] baumslag-gersten-port-route-fails-at-the-dilation-centralizer: PORTS MUST BE PARABOLIC. Power-conjugate cyclic edge groups are never cofinite ports (cycle counts), so BS-type HNN edges need rank>=2 ports, and the conjugating element must sit in the other port. In Thompson/odometer hosts that element is a shift, and by Curtis-Hedlund-Lyndon its centralizer is cyclic. So the port route to BG (and to the BS chains of 5.3(9)) fails at step 1 (the dilation-side port); faithfulness/generation never reached. Open gate: a host where the BS dilation is non-expansive.
- bh-free-57 (hyperbolic-measures-give-hyperbolic-periodic-points-in-ct-p-z, 861494c34): exact affine pieces make closing exact. A recurrent hyperbolic orbit segment closes to a periodic point of slope not 1, so hyperbolic dynamics certifies infinite order. Everything undecidable or exotic in CT(Z) (torsion, writers, minimal elements, intermediate growth) must live in the zero-exponent (isometric) part of some hyperbolic prime.
- bh-major-product [weak links] exotic-rank-two-building-lattices-are-mif (Le Bars–Leibtag–Vigdorovich 2609.17129, credit theirs): MIF on rank-2 buildings comes from OPPOSITION, not rank-one hyperbolicity — a hyperbolic element's domestic set is nowhere dense (girth-8 incidence argument). So PBH has two independent certificates, a nowhere-domestic boundary (MIF + BFFHZ C) and a host machine; every known fp simple group has one, TMW kernels have both. A Q3.4 counterexample needs neither. Also: Stein 1992 is not needed anywhere (Li k-graph theorem covers integral Stein V ⊇ T ⊇ Hyde–Lodha Q_n).
- bh-kourovka-2146 [7f25f45d8] tree-side-of-davis-amalgams-is-local-once-dense: DENSITY PROPAGATES ACROSS FACTORS. For a lattice amalgam W_1 *_A W_2 in a product, density of the X-projection (closure containing a simple group with trivial centralizer) forces faithfulness and non-discreteness on the tree. The tree side then asks only for finite permutation data, W_i acting on W_i/A containing Alt. So simplicity of such a host is three finite certificates plus one non-RF input, the right shape for a formal proof. Explicit torsion-free input with d = 6: the type-preserving index-4 subgroups of Radu's Gamma_{6,6,k}, which are simple.
- bh-invent-12 [460956b9d] finite-root-prime-splittings-never-create-z-semilocal: DIVISIBILITY IS SPLITTING-RIGID. Roots travel along a tree only through edge inclusions H ≤ B, and each one passes only the primes of its relative root indices min{i : v^i ∈ H}. With finitely many such primes, every Z_(S) sits in one vertex stabilizer (as Z_(S∪P)), never as an ascending union along a ray, and 'roots at finitely many primes' is closed under the splitting. This kills all Borisov–Sapir RF mapping tori of free-group endomorphisms (infinite-index images too) and graphs of free, abelian or nilpotent groups as (SL) witnesses, and it generalizes end-shift part 6 without RF. (SL) is a question about ONE irreducible piece, or about an infinitely root-deficient edge.
- bh-e3prime [a0adfac690] grigorchuk-schreier-tip-seeds-a-non-free-orbit: (IE′) IS A ONE-DEFECT PROBLEM; BRANCH STRUCTURE PINS TIPS, NOT POINTS. An SFT compactification is a one-defect SFT: some pattern occurs at most once per configuration, and the defect-free remainder is an SFT. The Grigorchuk group has a non-metric seed: 1^∞ is the only point fixed by b, c and d, so the fixed-generator label configuration of the Schreier-ray tip is isolated with dense orbit G/Stab(1^∞). The pinned object is a coset of the infinite parabolic subgroup, since finitely many boundary points never have trivial common stabilizer in a branch group; free seeds need a hierarchical code of tips at all levels. Growth alone does not rule out Cannon seeds: finitely many cone types constrain geodesic growth, not element growth. Open: finite type of the tip subshift over G, and a finitely generated parabolic splitting that puts G/P at an emitter.
- bh-g1-bss (ee75271f2 + correction e5d54e463): 'shared infinite-level degeneracy' is not an obstruction by itself. In aligned plane lifts floating data is LOCALLY a key function for ANY coupling; globally it can be neither constant (invariant factor ⇒ input SFT) nor switch at far places (separable) — only an exceptional-key defect (clustered in Λ_0, unbounded in Z^2, single infinite zone) survives. In transplant lifts shared degeneracy makes freedom plane-global, as rigidity wants. The Z^2 route is OPEN, not dead: decisive tests are defect realizability and (PF1)+skeleton rigidity for the Busemann height transplant.
- bh-g3-topfree [5b30cefdb] mihailova-relative-seeds-must-force-across-both-factors: MIHAILOVA CANNOT BE SEEDED ONE FACTOR AT A TIME. Rows of any configuration with stabilizer M(Q) ≤ F×F are translates of one row whose stabilizer has normal core ker(F→Q). That stabilizer is infinitely generated when Q is infinite and not free, while a free factor forces only fg stabilizers. So the relators' normal closure must be forced diagonally, which is the exact form of 'rules see relators, not conjugates'. Diagonals are seeded row by row when right multiplication acts on a rigid seed by cellular automata (F×F: the sink-moving automaton, so (F×F, Δ_F) ∈ 𝓡). That design already seeds Q, so the Mihailova rung helps only through two-dimensional forcing.
- bh-typeA-universal: Machine-universal maps are capped as groups; their universality lives in orbits, not words. Take any frame class whose n-fold composites have 2^O(n)-size descriptions with a polynomial identity test (finite transducers, Conway/RCWA/class transpositions, integer piecewise-affine maps). It gives hosts with single-exponential WP whatever their Ore condition. Residue-class operads even have unary squares, so Ore is automatic. The machine operad therefore cannot be assembled from a clever frame class: it IS a finite Ore Λ-substitution for a hard Λ, with the mortal machine in its Ore condition. — arithmetic-frames-are-capped-machines-live-in-orbits-not-words (252979610)
- [bh-free-60, a99547ddf] partial-torus-abels-quotients-are-isolated. CORRECTION to my d477f9e62 / cf708c5c7 lines: Abels hosts do NOT have max-n (A_4(Z[1/p]) has a centre that is not finitely normally generated, Abels LNM 1261 0.2.17), so 'all quotients of Abels groups' and 'fp and max-n' were wrong. Replacement lesson: ISOLATION NEEDS ONE INVISIBLE CORNER, NOT NOETHERIANITY. In unipotent-by-diagonal S-arithmetic hosts, commutators take every normal subgroup down to the corner, so a finitely cogenerated corner quotient makes the host isolated. Kneser's criterion (Abels 0.2.8) plus restriction of scalars gives finite presentation at completely split primes. So Prüfer radicals twisted by rational or irrational S-units (G_2, G_λ) have isolated hosts.
bh-free-09: CARRIER seeds pass the classical rigidity test. In distance-gradient (horofunction) seeds every freedom is a sink moving toward the point's own limit (no sources), so freedoms share a tail or a switch; proved for wall-chamber seeds (nested-chain orientations switch once ⇒ modifications within r_0+2), and preserved by products and relative-seed/Kurosh gluing (unique sink/end). Remaining risk: building wall strata where a field can change in two opposite directions at infinity (lines of opposite type) — rigid-seed-constructions-pass-the-separability-test (fe9b06f50)
- bh-major-cube-b [3d36b55b03] (with referee bh-ref-q115-a): DOUBLING GIVES LINK CONNECTIVITY BY DICKSON. With a Cuntz doubling move at every type and trivial units, the reachable type-multisets are eventually downward closed (Dickson), so any object with enough pieces admits a fresh doubling atom compatible with every given simplex: the link lemma needs no packing number at all. This replaces an unargued step in Li's written proof of lem:link:n-conn_3 (recorded neutrally; the framework is Li's). Also: coding-exit lemmas must pass every hole's witness wall before exiting (Lemma E-prime).
- bh-g1-universal-point [3e6dfac7f] digit-paths-only-transport-the-halting-core-mark: FREE EDGE GROUPS TRANSPORT; GRIDS COMPUTE. An HNN edge coset meets the base in one coset of the trace (E ∩ H_1 = P). Via Kurosh normal form, "core" is syllable-wise membership plus ONE predicate at the last syllable, constant on the trace. So a two-sided core mark needs no computation in the edge group (no Z², and tree readers are sliding-block). The halting pair's whole CAP content is the single K_M predicate [g ∈ ⟨U⟩P]: the machine subgroup against the rope.
- bh-emitter-a isolated-order-word-problems-are-bounded-by-positive-distortion: AN ISOLATED ORDER IS AS HARD AS ITS POSITIVE DISTORTION. With an fg positive cone, short positive words certify g != 1, so WP is in NTIME and coNTIME[poly(D_P, Dehn)]. A CAP counterexample there needs super-polynomial positive distortion or area. Regularity of the cone is automatic and forces nothing. Higman free-product stages admit no isolated order (Rivas).
- [bh-invent-06b nucleus-lag] CONTRACTING DOES NOT BOUND LAG. The map p(01ω)=0p(ω), p(1ω)=10ω, p(00ω)=11ω on the 2-shift lies in a full contracting RSG with the five-element nucleus {id, p, r, p⁻¹, s} (checked by hand, all six BBMZ conditions). Its nucleus cycle along (01)^∞ has weight −1 and moves [(01)^∞] to [0^∞]. So tail balance holds only for returning tails. Lag can be transferred between tail classes inside contracting hosts, and conservativity for asynchronous hosts cannot come from a uniform lag bound.
